- `MarietteKernel::ComputeIO` derives `GroupQueryAttentionParams` (scale, rotary_dim from
  `cos.shape[-1]*2`, `past_seq = past_k.shape[2]`, `present_seq = past_seq + S`) and dispatches
  `MetalContext::GroupQueryAttention`, which resolves every operand to a device buffer
  (LookupBuffer for device-resident tensors, else a wrapped host temp) and encodes the two passes.

## Two bugs found & fixed while landing this

1. **Schema-default attributes made the claim silently fail.** ORT materializes op schema defaults;
   `GetAttributeByName` returns *absent* optional INT attrs as type INT with the default — for this
   node `smooth_softmax = -1`, `local_window_size = -1`. The original `smooth_softmax != 0` guard
   therefore rejected **every** GQA node (claimed 366/393, 27 subgraphs, unchanged). Fixed to test
   the *enabled* sentinel (`== 1`) and hardened `IntAttribute`/`FloatAttribute` to validate
   `GetType()` before reading. (Repository memory stored.)
2. **Fused-path garbage from a host read of a device tensor.** `seqlens_k` / `total_sequence_length`
   are `Cast` outputs, and `Cast` is Metal-claimed — so in the fused subgraph they are
   **device-resident**, and the host dispatch reading `seqlens_k[b]` to size the write grid got a
   stale host shadow → whole-decoder output was garbage from token 0. Fixed by sizing the write grid
   and rotary-cache wrap from `present_seq` (shape-derived) and letting the **kernel** read
   `seqlens_k` on-device for per-batch validity. After the fix the fused stream equals the
   non-fused stream exactly. (Repository memory stored.)

## Correctness (GQA-specific, verified FIRST)

- **C++ CPU-reference** (`tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc`, `TestGroupQueryAttention`, tol 2e-3):
  8 cases — decode, decode-norope, decode-batch, prefill, prefill-interleaved, chunked, decode-SWA,
  prefill-SWA. **PASS.**
- **Differential vs ORT CPU-EP through the EP** (`tests/kernels/ort_reference_test.py`, rtol/atol 2e-3):
  13 cases including single-node decode/prefill/chunked/SWA/batch **and** multi-node fused
  (`Add → GQA → Add`, so GQA consumes/produces device-resident intermediates) at both toy dims and
  the real head=64/num_heads=14 geometry. **PASS.**
- **E2E** (`tests/e2e/mps_e2e`, growing KV cache): Metal-GQA token stream is **byte-identical to the
  CPU-GQA baseline** for all 64 tokens (verified by diffing the two runs). i.e. swapping CPU GQA for
  Metal GQA changes *nothing* in the output — the kernel is numerically faithful.
- ctest: `mps_kernel_tests`, `mps_mariette_tests`, `mps_ort_reference_tests` → **3/3 PASS**.

## Remaining gap (NOT GQA — pre-existing, out of scope)

The strict E2E coherence gate (full stream == pure CPU) still diverges at ~token 14. This is the
**known pre-existing fp32 reduction-order drift** flagged in `nabil-batched-dispatch.md`
("8-token coherence is the bar … ≥~14 tokens diverges … not dispatch"), rooted in `MatMulNBits`
(int8 `accuracy_level=4` on CPU vs fp32 dequant on Metal — see `sebastian-accuracy-level-debug.md`).
Proof it is not GQA: with GQA forced back to CPU the baseline diverges at the **same** token 14, and
Metal-GQA reproduces the CPU-GQA stream exactly. The **8-token gate PASSES**. Closing the pure-CPU
gap is a `MatMulNBits`-precision task (coordinate with Coco/Sebastian), independent of this kernel.

Perf headroom for a later pass: Pass-2 currently keeps `acc[T*HS]` in threadgroup memory; a
register-tiled variant + fp16 K/V loads should narrow the gap to llama.cpp Metal (~200).

## Files changed (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)
- `src/kernels/gqa.metal` — **new**: `mps_gqa_write_kv_f32`, `mps_gqa_attention_f32`, `gqa_rope_value`.
- `src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}` — `GroupQueryAttentionParams` + `MetalContext::GroupQueryAttention`
  (two-pass single-encoder dispatch; device/host operand resolution; T selection; inter-pass barrier).
- `src/ep/ep.{h,cc}` — GQA claim in `MarietteClaimable`; attr parse + `ComputeIO` branch in
  `MarietteKernel`; `IntAttribute`/`FloatAttribute` type-guarded against schema-default phantoms.
- `tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc` — `GqaReference` + 8 CPU-ref cases.
- `tests/kernels/ort_reference_test.py` — 13 GQA differential cases (single-node + fused, head=16/64).
- `CMakeLists.txt` — add `src/kernels/gqa.metal` to the Metal kernel list.

## Reproduce
```bash
cd onnxruntime-mps/build && cmake . && cmake --build . -j8
ORTLIB=<onnx-genai>/target/release/build/onnx-genai-ort-sys-*/out/ort-prebuilt/lib
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ctest --output-on-failure
# E2E (coherence + tok/s + subgraph/commit counts):
ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_PROFILE=1 DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./mps_e2e \
  <onnx-genai>/models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx \
  "$PWD/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib" ../tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8   # gate PASS at 8
```


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/mariette-hot-kernels.md -->
# Decision: Metal decode hot-path kernels (MatMulNBits, RMSNorm, SkipSimplifiedLayerNorm, Softmax)

**Author:** Mariette (Metal/MPS Kernel Engineer)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Repo:** onnxruntime-mps
**Status:** Implemented, correctness-proven (E2E coherent + per-kernel vs CPU). Perf: see caveat.

## Scope

Implemented the compute-heavy decode kernels for the Metal EP, registered via Nabil's
compile-based dispatch and kept merge-isolated from Coco's parallel work (elementwise/gather/rope)
by mirroring Coco's separation pattern: a dedicated `MarietteKernel` struct + `mariette_kernels_`
map + `MarietteClaimable()` + `MarietteNodeComputeInfo`, added additively alongside (never
replacing) the Add/Coco registrations.

Kernels (own `.metal` files, loaded into the runtime-compiled library via CMake
`ORT_MPS_METAL_KERNEL_FILES` → `metal_kernels.inc`):

- **MatMulNBits** (`com.microsoft`, 169 nodes) — THE decode bottleneck.
- **SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization** (`com.microsoft`, 48 nodes).
- **RMSNormalization** (`ai.onnx`, 1 node).
- **Softmax** (`ai.onnx`, 0 in this graph — unit-tested only).

## MatMulNBits design

int4 block-quantized weights, `bits=4`, `block_size=32`, 3 inputs (no zero_points → symmetric
default zp=8). Packing (ORT contrib, verified by per-kernel test + E2E coherence): B is
`[N, nblocks, 16]` uint8; within a 32-element block byte = k/2, **low nibble = even k**, high
nibble = odd k; `w[n][k] = (q - 8) * scale[n][block]`; `Y = A · dequant(B)^T` (+ optional bias).

- **Decode GEMV (M=1, memory-bound — the important case):** one 32-lane simdgroup per output
  column n; grid.x = N*32, grid.y = M, threadgroup = 256. Lane L owns within-block index L, sweeps
  all `nblocks` blocks reading 16 packed bytes + 32 activations coalesced, dequantizes, accumulates
  in fp32, then `simd_sum` reduces the 32 partials to Y[m,n]. All graph N are multiples of 8 so
  N*32 tiles the 256-wide threadgroup exactly and simdgroups stay full.
- **Prefill GEMM (M>1):** handled by the same kernel looping over M rows (grid.y = M). Correct;
  a tiled `simdgroup_matrix` GEMM is a future perf upgrade for large-M prefill.
- **Accuracy:** currently fp32 accumulation (matches the fp32 cpu-recipe graph and passes
  correctness). The `accuracy_level=4` int8-dynamic-quant + int8 dot-product fast path
  (llama.cpp `mul_mv_q`-style) is the next perf step; deferred to keep correctness first.

**Key perf lever — device-resident weights:** the constant B (uint8) + scales (fp32) are copied
into `MetalContext::Alloc`'d device buffers **once** on the first `Compute` and reused every decode
step (cached in the `MarietteKernel` object; freed in its dtor). Because Alloc'd pointers are
tracked in the context's buffer table, `LookupBuffer` resolves them zero-copy. This avoids
re-uploading ~350MB of weights per token. No changes to Coco's/Nabil's `Impl` struct.

## Norm kernels

- **RMSNormalization:** one threadgroup per row, `simd_sum` + threadgroup scratch reduction of
  sum(x²); `y = x / sqrt(mean(x²)+eps) * gamma`, fp32.
- **SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization:** residual = input + skip (written to the last output when
  ORT keeps it — the fusion drops the unused mean/inv_std_var outputs, so we write out[0] =
  normalized always and out[count-1] = residual when output count > 1), then RMS-normalize.

## Correctness

- **Per-kernel** (`tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc`, target `mps_mariette_tests`): MatMulNBits
  (M=1 GEMV and M=3 GEMM), RMSNorm, SkipLN (normalized + residual), Softmax all match a scalar CPU
  reference. Tolerances: 1e-3 (matmul/norm, fp32 GPU reduction order), 1e-5 (softmax). **PASS.**
- **E2E** (`mps_e2e`, Qwen2.5-0.5B cpu-recipe, greedy): MetalEP token stream ==
  CPU token stream = `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645` ("... is Paris."). **COHERENCE PASS.**
  With Coco's kernels also claimed, **366 / 393 nodes run on Metal** (72 Add + 169 MatMulNBits +
  48 SkipLN + 1 RMS + Coco's elementwise/gather/rope; remaining 27 fall back to CPU).

## Perf caveat (important, needs coordination)

Decode with the full graph on Metal is currently **~11.8 tok/s vs ~166 tok/s CPU** — a *loss*, not
a win, despite correct kernels. Root cause is **architectural, not per-kernel**: the shared dispatch
helpers (`Dispatch` in Coco's path, `RunPass` in mine) each do a full `commit` +
`waitUntilCompleted` + host memcpy copy-back **per node**. At 366 nodes/token that is ~366 GPU
round-trips + host copies per token, which serializes everything and dwarfs the actual compute.

The MatMulNBits kernel itself is correct and the weights are device-resident; the win it should
deliver is masked by per-node synchronization. **To realize Metal decode speedup we need a
framework change (shared, coordinate with Nabil + Coco):** batch a token's dispatches into one
command buffer with a single `waitUntilCompleted`, and keep intermediate tensors device-resident
(skip copy-back when an output feeds another Metal node) instead of round-tripping to host between
every op. That is the highest-leverage next step. After that, adding the int8 `accuracy_level=4`
fast path to MatMulNBits is the second lever to approach llama.cpp Metal.

## Files

Created: `src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal`, `src/kernels/norm.metal`, `src/kernels/softmax.metal`,
`tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc`.
Modified (all additive, merge-safe): `src/ep/ep.h`, `src/ep/ep.cc`, `src/ep/metal_context.h`,
`src/ep/metal_context.mm`, `CMakeLists.txt`.

## Build / test / run

```
cmake -S . -B build -G "Unix Makefiles" && cmake --build build -j8
./build/mps_mariette_tests            # per-kernel vs CPU reference
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=<ort-prebuilt>/lib ./build/mps_e2e \
  <qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe>/model.onnx $PWD/build/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib \
  tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8       # E2E coherence + tok/s
```

Note: Metal helper functions share one runtime-compiled program source, so helper names must be
globally unique across all `.metal` files (hit a `tg_reduce_sum` redefinition between norm.metal
and softmax.metal → renamed to `mps_norm_reduce_sum` / `mps_softmax_reduce_{sum,max}`).


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/mariette-matmulnbits-bandwidth.md -->
# MatMulNBits decode GEMV — vectorized uint4 weight loads make fp32 the fast path (~106 → ~133 tok/s, +27%)

**By:** Mariette (Metal/MPS Kernel Engineer)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, macOS 25.x (~400 GB/s theoretical UMA bandwidth)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` (Metal EP). Model: `onnx-genai/models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx`
(fp32 graph, 169 MatMulNBits, whole decoder = 1 fused subgraph = 1 commit/token).
**No commit — coordinator commits.**

## TL;DR

Decode is weight-bandwidth-bound (`mariette-matmulnbits-int8`): each token streams the full packed
int4 weight set once. The previous default kernel read those weights **one byte at a time** (with two
adjacent simd lanes hitting the *same* byte) — narrow 8-bit loads, only 16 weight-bytes moved per
simdgroup per loop step, so it never approached achievable memory throughput. Rewriting the load path
to **wide, fully-coalesced `uint4` (128-bit) transactions** — each lane owns a whole 32-element block
and the 32 lanes of a simdgroup read 32 consecutive blocks = **512 contiguous bytes** per step — lifts
steady decode from **~106 → ~133 tok/s (+~27%)**, with best runs momentarily matching the CPU EP
(~156). This is now the **default**. fp16-math on top gave **no** further gain (confirming the kernel
is bandwidth-bound, not ALU-bound) and adds a small quant error, so it stays opt-in.

| Metal MatMulNBits path (64-tok decode, steady; M1 Max) | decode tok/s | 8-tok gate |
|---|---:|:--:|
| scalar byte-load (`mps_matmulnbits_f32`, prior default) | ~106 | PASS |
| **uint4 wide-load fp32 (`mps_matmulnbits_f32_v`, NEW default)** | **~132–135 (peak ~156)** | **PASS** |
| uint4 wide-load fp16-math (`mps_matmulnbits_f16_v`, opt-in) | ~133 (no gain) | PASS |
| — CPU EP reference (same box) | ~153–159 | — |
| — llama.cpp Metal (reference) | ~200 | — |

Cold-start first run is a warmup outlier (~78 tok/s) and excluded from the steady median.

## The bandwidth fix (`src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal`)

New `mps_matmulnbits_f32_v` (dispatch identical to the old kernel: one 32-lane simdgroup per output
column `n`, grid = N*32 × M, 256-wide threadgroup = 8 columns):

- **Wide coalesced weight loads.** `B` is column-major `[nblocks][16 bytes]`, 16-byte aligned per
  column. Each lane loads a whole block's 16 packed bytes as a single **`uint4`** and strides
  `for (b = lane; b < nblocks; b += 32)`, so consecutive lanes always read consecutive blocks →
  the simdgroup issues one 512-byte contiguous burst per step (llama.cpp `kernel_mul_mv_*` layout),
  no redundant loads, no byte-granularity accesses.
- Activations read as **`float4`** (128-bit, aligned since `block_size==32` ⇒ K multiple of 4).
  Nibbles are unpacked into `float4`s and reduced with `dot()`; one **`simd_sum`** at the very end
  (each lane summed its complete blocks) — no per-block reduction.
- **fp32 accumulate** to hold the ORT CPU tolerance on long-K reductions.

`mps_matmulnbits_f16_v` is identical but converts the activation to `half` and multiplies in fp16
(fp32 per-block promote for the cross-block reduction). Weight bytes are byte-identical (the dominant
traffic is unchanged), so it only touches the ALU — and on this bandwidth-bound kernel that buys
nothing. Kept behind a flag for any future ALU-bound / lower-weight-byte regime.

The scalar `mps_matmulnbits_f32` (byte loads) is retained unchanged as the **correctness reference**.

## Occupancy / threadgroup sweep

Swept threads-per-threadgroup ∈ {64,128,256,512} (= 2/4/8/16 output columns per group) via
`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_TG`. 256 and 512 were best and statistically tied (peaks ~156); 64 was
noisier with a low tail. **Kept 256** (8 columns/group) as the tuned default — the register/occupancy
sweet spot and consistent with the other Mariette kernels. Env knob left in place (defaults to 256)
for future tuning.

## Wiring (`src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}`, `src/ep/ep.cc`)

`MetalContext::MatMulNBitsF32` gained a defaulted `MatMulNBitsVariant {Auto,Scalar,VectorF32,VectorF16}`
argument. `Auto` (production) = **VectorF32** unless overridden by env:
`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_SCALAR=1` (reference), `…_FP16=1` (fp16 math),
`…_TG=<mult-of-32>` (threadgroup width). `ep.cc` is unchanged in behavior — the same call now hits the
vectorized kernel by default; constant weights remain device-resident (copied once). The `int8` path
(`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_INT8`) is orthogonal and unchanged.

## Correctness (verified FIRST)

- **Per-kernel** (`tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc`, `TestMatMulNBits`, target
  `mps_mariette_tests`): all 3 variants at M∈{1,2,3} and **K∈{64, 1088}** — K=1088 = 34 blocks
  exercises the `b += 32` block-stride wrap and the ragged tail (>1 simdgroup of blocks, not a
  multiple of 32). vs the fp32 CPU reference: scalar & **VectorF32 exact within 1e-3**;
  **VectorF16 worst abs-err = 0.0031** (K=1088), bounded at 5e-2 (fp16-aware). 
- **ctest**: `mps_kernel_tests`, `mps_mariette_tests`, `mps_ort_reference_tests` → **3/3 PASS**
  (ort-reference differential still exact under the new default).
- **E2E** (`mps_e2e`, Qwen2.5-0.5B cpu-recipe, greedy): **8-token COHERENCE GATE PASS** for both the
  default (vec-fp32) and the fp16 opt-in — stream `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645`
  == CPU ("The capital of France is Paris."). Fluent + correct. (The known pre-existing ~token-14
  fp32 reduction-order drift beyond 8 tokens is unchanged and out of scope —
  `mariette-gqa-kernel.md`, `nabil-batched-dispatch.md`.)

## Bandwidth estimate

Effective weight bandwidth ≈ decode tok/s × ~350 MB/token (whole-token wall clock, so a *lower bound*
on the kernel's true throughput since a token also spends time in GQA/norms/softmax/rope + the
per-token commit/wait):
- scalar prior default: 106 × 350 MB ≈ **37 GB/s** (~9% of 400 GB/s theoretical)
- **new vec-fp32 default: 133 × 350 MB ≈ 46–47 GB/s** (~12% of theoretical); peaks (~156) ≈ 55 GB/s

The +27% tok/s tracks the +27% effective bandwidth — i.e. the win is purely moving the weight bytes
more efficiently, exactly as predicted. The whole-token figure sits well under 400 GB/s because
weight streaming is only a fraction of per-token wall time; the MatMulNBits kernel itself sustains
materially higher, and the remaining headroom is non-kernel overhead.

## Remaining gap to CPU (~153) / llama.cpp Metal (~200)

Now at **~87% of CPU** and **~66% of llama.cpp Metal** (was ~69% / ~53%). What's left is *not* the
weight-load path:
- **per-token `waitUntilCompleted`** on the single fused subgraph — overlap encode/execute across the
  token boundary (double-buffer command buffers) to hide submit/sync latency;
- **non-MatMulNBits per-token time** (GQA, norms, softmax, rope, elementwise) now a larger relative
  share — profile with `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_PROFILE` to find the next dominant node;
- possible further MatMulNBits micro-opt: 2-columns-per-lane to reuse each loaded activation across
  outputs, or `simdgroup_matrix` tiling for large-M prefill (separate from decode).
- fp16 **activations end-to-end** (graph dtype) would finally cut activation *device* traffic — but
  activation is KB-scale and L2-resident, so the payoff is small vs the weight stream.

## Files changed (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)
- `src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal` — **new** `mps_matmulnbits_f32_v` (uint4 wide-load, fp32) and
  `mps_matmulnbits_f16_v` (uint4 wide-load, fp16 math); scalar `mps_matmulnbits_f32` unchanged.
- `src/ep/metal_context.mm` — register both pipelines; `MatMulNBitsF32` variant selector + env
  overrides (`…_SCALAR`, `…_FP16`, `…_TG`); default = VectorF32.
- `src/ep/metal_context.h` — `MatMulNBitsVariant` enum + defaulted `variant` param + doc.
- `tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc` — `TestMatMulNBits` covers all 3 variants at K∈{64,1088}
  (block-stride + tail) and quantifies the fp16 error.

## Reproduce
```bash
cd onnxruntime-mps && cmake --build build -j8
ORTLIB=$(dirname $(ls <onnx-genai>/target/release/build/onnx-genai-ort-sys-*/out/ort-prebuilt/lib/libonnxruntime.dylib|head -1))
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure          # 3/3 PASS
MODEL=<onnx-genai>/models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx
EP=$PWD/build/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./build/mps_e2e "$MODEL" "$EP" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8   # gate PASS
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./build/mps_e2e "$MODEL" "$EP" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 64  # tok/s
# A/B: force scalar reference, fp16 math, or a threadgroup width
ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_SCALAR=1 …   # prior byte-load kernel (~106)
ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_FP16=1   …   # fp16 math (~133, +small err)
ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_TG=512   …   # occupancy sweep
```


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/mariette-matmulnbits-int8.md -->
# MatMulNBits int8 dot-product fast path (Metal) — implemented & correct, but decode GEMV is weight-bandwidth-bound so fp32 stays default

**By:** Mariette (Metal/MPS Kernel Engineer)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, macOS 25.x
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` (Metal EP). Model: `onnx-genai/models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx` (fp32, 169 MatMulNBits).
**No commit — coordinator commits.**

## TL;DR

Added the int8 dynamic-quant fast path (accuracy_level=4 scheme, llama.cpp `mul_mv_q`-style) to the
Metal `MatMulNBits` kernel. It is **numerically faithful** (GPU int8 == scalar CPU int8 reference
exactly; worst-case error vs fp32 quantified below) and **coherent E2E** (8-token gate PASS). But on
the Apple GPU it is **~20% slower** than the existing fp32 dequant path for decode, because **decode
GEMV (M=1) is weight-bandwidth-bound, not compute-bound**. Both paths read the identical 4-bit packed
weight bytes (~350 MB, the dominant traffic); int8 only adds activation-quant + threadgroup-memory
occupancy overhead without cutting the bottleneck. This is the **opposite** of CPU, where
`accuracy_level=4` won 2.3× precisely because the ARM path was **SDOT-compute-bound**
(`sebastian-accuracy-level-debug.md`).

**Decision: keep fp32 as the default Metal decode path; ship the int8 kernel behind an opt-in
(`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_INT8=1`), correctness-proven, for compute-bound / large-M or future
lower-bandwidth-weight regimes.**

| Metal MatMulNBits path (64-tok decode, steady) | decode tok/s | 8-tok gate |
|---|---:|:--:|
| **fp32 dequant (default)** | **~101–104** | **PASS** |
| int8 dynamic-quant (`…_INT8=1`) | ~80–85 | PASS |
| — CPU EP reference (same box) | ~150–160 | — |
| — llama.cpp Metal (reference) | ~200 | — |

Baseline going into this task was 98.6 (GQA decision, 64-tok); fp32 default measures ~101–104 here
(warmup/measurement noise) — i.e. **unchanged/slightly better**, no regression.

## int8 GEMV design (`src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal` → `mps_matmulnbits_i8`)

Single **fused** kernel (no separate quantize pass, no device scratch, no cross-pass barrier):

- Dispatch = same as fp32: one 32-lane simdgroup per output column `n`; grid.x = N*32, grid.y = M,
  threadgroup = 256 (⇒ **8 columns per threadgroup, all sharing one activation row** for M=1 decode).
- **Phase A (once per threadgroup):** the 8 simdgroups cooperatively dynamically-quantize the shared
  activation row to int8 into **threadgroup memory** — per K-block (block_size=32) symmetric absmax
  scale `a_scale = absmax/127`, `qa = clamp(round(a/ a_scale), -127, 127)`. `simd_max(|a|)` gives the
  block absmax; blocks are striped across the 8 simdgroups. `threadgroup_barrier` then publishes it.
  Quantizing once per 8 columns (vs once per column) is the amortization llama.cpp uses.
- **Phase B:** each simdgroup sweeps its column's blocks: unpack int4 weight → signed `(q-8)` in
  [-8,7], `prod = int(qa[k]) * wq`, **int32** `simd_sum(prod)` per block, then
  `acc += float(block_dot) * (a_scale[b] * w_scale[b])`; fp32 across blocks. Lane 0 writes Y (+bias).
- Handles M>1 (prefill) with the same kernel (grid.y = M). Not a tiled `simdgroup_matrix` GEMM — the
  large-M prefill tiling remains future work (as before).
- fp32 reference/fallback path `mps_matmulnbits_f32` kept unchanged (now the default).

Wiring (`src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}`, `src/ep/ep.cc`): new `MetalContext::MatMulNBitsI8` encodes the
one fused dispatch (threadgroup-memory lengths for `tg_qa[K]` + `tg_ascale[nblocks]`), batch-aware
like the other kernels; `MarietteKernel::ComputeIO` selects int8 only when
`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_INT8` is set, else fp32. Constant weights stay device-resident (unchanged).

## Why int8 doesn't win on the GPU (the measured finding)

Decode is **M=1 GEMV**: for each of 169 nodes the kernel streams the full 4-bit weight matrix and
does a tiny amount of math per weight. It is **memory-bandwidth-bound on the weights**, which are
**byte-identical** between fp32 and int8 (same packed uint8 `[N,nblocks,16]`). int8 therefore:
- does **not** reduce the dominant memory traffic (weights), and the activation is KB-scale (irrelevant);
- **adds** work: a `simd_max` reduction + quantize per block, plus threadgroup memory that lowers GPU
  occupancy (the head's K=896 ⇒ ~1 KB threadgroup/block, fewer resident threadgroups than the
  zero-threadgroup-memory fp32 kernel).

So int8's CPU win (SDOT throughput on a compute-bound kernel) does not transfer to this GPU regime.
Confirmed by 3× repeated A/B: int8 80–85 vs fp32 101–104 tok/s.

## Correctness (verified FIRST — precision-sensitive)

- **Per-kernel** (`tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc`, `TestMatMulNBitsInt8`, target
  `mps_mariette_tests`): M=1 and M=4, N=16, K=256. Two references:
  - vs a scalar **CPU int8** reference applying the identical per-block absmax quant → **exact match**
    (`worst |GPU-int8 − CPU-int8| = 0`). Proves the kernel implements accuracy_level=4 math faithfully.
  - vs the **fp32** dequant reference (the intrinsic quantization error): **worst 3.72% relative**
    (on |ref|≥0.1) / **0.017 absolute**. Gate = combined atol+rtol (`0.05 + 0.05·|ref|`); pure
    relative error is meaningless on near-zero cancellation outputs. **PASS.**
- **ctest**: `mps_kernel_tests`, `mps_mariette_tests`, `mps_ort_reference_tests` → **3/3 PASS** (default
  fp32; ort-reference differential still exact).
- **E2E** (`mps_e2e`, Qwen2.5-0.5B cpu-recipe, greedy, whole decoder = 1 fused subgraph, 1 commit/token):
  8-token stream == CPU = `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645` ("... is Paris.") for **both** default
  fp32 **and** `…_INT8=1`. **8-token COHERENCE GATE PASS** both ways. (The known pre-existing ~token-14
  fp32 reduction-order drift is unchanged and out of scope — see `mariette-gqa-kernel.md`,
  `nabil-batched-dispatch.md`.)

## Remaining gap to CPU (~150–160) / llama.cpp Metal (~200)

Not MatMulNBits arithmetic. The decode is weight-bandwidth-bound and fp32 already sits near that bound
(~103 tok/s). To close on llama.cpp the levers are memory/dispatch, not int8 activation quant:
- **fp16 activations + fp16 accumulation** (halve activation traffic, native M-series fp16 throughput);
- **overlap encode/execute** across the 1 subgraph boundary (remove the per-token `waitUntilCompleted`);
- weight-read micro-opt (vectorized `uint4` loads of the packed bytes; better cache/coalescing);
- large-M prefill tiled `simdgroup_matrix` GEMM (separate from decode).
int8 would only help if a future weight format reduced weight bytes or a genuinely compute-bound
regime appeared (large-M GEMM) — the kernel is ready for that behind the flag.

## Files changed (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)
- `src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal` — **new** fused `mps_matmulnbits_i8` (threadgroup-cooperative int8
  quant + int32 dot); `mps_matmulnbits_f32` unchanged.
- `src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}` — `MatMulNBitsI8` (single fused dispatch, threadgroup memory);
  register `mps_matmulnbits_i8` pipeline.
- `src/ep/ep.cc` — `MarietteKernel::ComputeIO` selects fp32 (default) vs int8 via
  `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_INT8`.
- `tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc` — `TestMatMulNBitsInt8` (vs CPU-int8 exact + vs fp32 error
  quantification).

## Reproduce
```bash
cd onnxruntime-mps && cmake --build build -j8
ORTLIB=<onnx-genai>/target/release/build/onnx-genai-ort-sys-*/out/ort-prebuilt/lib
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure          # 3/3 PASS
# default fp32 vs int8 opt-in (8-tok gate + tok/s):
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./build/mps_e2e <model>/model.onnx \
  "$PWD/build/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8      # gate PASS
ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MATMUL_INT8=1 DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./build/mps_e2e ... 8   # gate PASS
```


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/mariette-prefill-gemm.md -->
# MatMulNBits prefill GEMM (simdgroup_matrix MMA) — Metal now BEATS CPU on TTFT at 256/512-token prompts (unblocks the hybrid)

**By:** Mariette (Metal/MPS Kernel Engineer)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, macOS 25.x (~400 GB/s theoretical UMA bandwidth)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` (Metal EP). Model: `onnx-genai/models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx`
(fp32 graph, 169 MatMulNBits, whole decoder = 1 fused subgraph).
**No commit — coordinator commits.**

## TL;DR

Batty measured (and I reproduced exactly) that Metal prefill/TTFT was **1.5–2× SLOWER than CPU,
gap growing with prompt length** — because the Metal `MatMulNBits` kernel was a **decode GEMV
replicated once per prompt row** (`grid.y = M`, no reuse): prefill re-streamed the full int4 weight
set M times = M×-weight-bandwidth-bound, the GPU's weakness.

I added a **tiled prefill GEMM** (`mps_matmulnbits_gemm_f32`) using **`simdgroup_matrix`
(simdgroup_float8x8) MMA** ops that loads each weight tile into threadgroup memory **once** and
reuses it across the M rows of the tile (compute-bound, where the GPU wins). Decode (M=1) is
untouched. Auto-routed for **M ≥ 8**.

Result — **Metal prefill now BEATS CPU at 256 and 512-token prompts** and is 1.7–2.2× faster than
the old GEMV-replicated prefill at every length. **This unblocks Batty's GPU-prefill/CPU-decode
hybrid** (the KV-handoff seam is already proven).

| prompt tokens | CPU TTFT | **Metal GEMM (NEW)** | old Metal GEMV | Metal/CPU | GEMM vs old GEMV |
|---|---:|---:|---:|:--:|:--:|
| 26   | ~33 ms   | **~57 ms**   | ~63 ms   | 1.73× (CPU) | 1.1× faster |
| 256  | ~276 ms  | **~242 ms**  | ~507 ms  | **0.88× — Metal FASTER** | 2.1× faster |
| 512  | ~570 ms  | **~560 ms**  | ~1099 ms | **0.98× — Metal FASTER** | 2.0× faster |
| 1024 | ~1271 ms | **~1522 ms** | ~2631 ms | 1.20× (CPU) | 1.7× faster |

(Best-of-N prefill-only forward, empty KV; my NOGEMM baseline reproduces Batty's old-Metal numbers
26/256/512/1024 = 66/525/1128/2756 ms to within thermals.) Metal wins the 256–512 sweet spot; at
26 it stays overhead-bound (tiny M), and at 1024 the remaining deficit is dominated by the O(M²)
attention (GQA) kernel, not MatMulNBits — a separate lever.

## The GEMM kernel (`src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal`, new `mps_matmulnbits_gemm_f32`)

Structure mirrors the Apple gold standard (llama.cpp `ggml-metal.metal` `kernel_mul_mm` +
`dequantize_q4_*`) — reimplemented, not vendored.

- **Tiling:** BM=32, BN=32, BK=32. Threadgroup = 128 threads = **4 simdgroups in a 2×2 grid**, each
  owning a 16×16 output subtile = **2×2 array of 8×8 fp32 accumulators**. Each staged weight tile is
  reused across all 32 rows of the tile ⇒ a length-M prefill reads the weight set ~**M/32 times**
  instead of M times.
- **K-loop = one quant block per step.** BK == block_size == 32, so each K-step maps to exactly one
  scale per column — clean dequant with no intra-tile block bookkeeping. Stage `As[32×32]` (fp16
  activations) and `Bs[32×32]` (fp16 **dequantized** weights, `(q−8)·scale`) into threadgroup memory
  cooperatively (128 threads), one `threadgroup_barrier` before the MMA and one after.
- **MMA:** `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate(acc, Afrag, Bfrag, acc)`. `Bfrag` is loaded **transposed**
  so the 8×8 fragment is Bᵀ (indices `[k][n]`), giving `C[m,n] = Σ_k A[m,k]·B_deq[n,k]`. **fp16
  tiles, fp32 accumulate** (`simdgroup_float8x8`) — the llama.cpp precision recipe.
- **Store:** accumulators → threadgroup scratch → bounds-checked + bias copy-out to device. M/N need
  not be tile multiples (staging zero-pads; store bounds-checks).

**Tile sweep:** BM=64/BN=32 (the llama.cpp 8-accumulator layout) was measured **slower** here
(256: 314 vs 242 ms) — the extra register/threadgroup pressure cut occupancy more than the halved
weight traffic helped. **32×32 with 128 threads is the M1 occupancy sweet spot** and is kept.

## Dispatch selection (`src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}`, `src/ep/ep.cc`)

- New `MatMulNBitsVariant::GemmF32`. In `MatMulNBitsF32`, `Auto` now routes **M ≥ threshold →
  GemmF32** (threadgroups `⌈N/32⌉ × ⌈M/32⌉`, 128 threads/group), else the tuned **VectorF32 GEMV**
  (decode, M=1). `ep.cc` passes the runtime M; **decode M=1 is byte-for-byte the same dispatch as
  before — untouched.**
- Env knobs: `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_GEMM_THRESHOLD=<int>` (default 8);
  `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_NOGEMM=1` forces the old GEMV-replicated prefill (for A/B measurement — this
  is how I reproduced the old numbers). Orthogonal to the existing `_SCALAR` / `_FP16` / `_INT8`
  knobs.

## Correctness (verified FIRST)

- **Per-kernel** (`tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc`, new `TestMatMulNBitsGemm`, target
  `mps_mariette_tests`): forces `GemmF32` at **M ∈ {8, 32, 128}** and at **ragged M/N** (40×96,
  100×48, 8×40 — not tile multiples, exercising the boundary padding + bounds-checked store) and
  **K up to 1088** (34 blocks). vs the fp32 CPU reference: **worst abs-err 0.00295, worst rel-err
  1.74 %** (|ref|≥0.1) — fp16-tile-bounded, well inside the combined atol+rtol gate.
- **ctest**: `mps_kernel_tests`, `mps_mariette_tests`, `mps_ort_reference_tests` → **3/3 PASS**.
- **E2E coherence** (`mps_e2e`, real 26-tok "capital of France" prompt, greedy, 8-tok gate):
  **PASS** — Metal stream `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645` == CPU ("The capital of France is
  Paris."). At padded prompts the gate can show FAIL, but the **GEMM Metal stream is byte-identical
  to the old-GEMV Metal stream** (both diverge from CPU at the same token) — i.e. it is the known
  pre-existing Metal fp32-dequant vs CPU int8 `accuracy_level=4` drift, **not** a GEMM defect.

## Decode unchanged

M=1 routes to the same `VectorF32` GEMV (threshold 8) — the decode hot path and its ~133 tok/s
steady figure (`mariette-matmulnbits-bandwidth`) are untouched; no code in that kernel changed.

## Safety (post-leak-fix discipline)

The GEMM path adds **no new device allocation**: it reuses the already-resolved operand buffers
(weights are device-resident from `ep.cc`'s one-time `Alloc`, freed in `~MarietteKernel`; any
wrapped host temporaries are released by `RunPass` exactly as the other kernels). All GEMM scratch
is **static threadgroup memory** (auto, ~8 KB: As 2 KB + Bs 2 KB + Cs 4 KB) — no unbounded
allocation, no new `-release` obligation. Tested with **bounded** runs (4 prompt lengths, ≤3 prefill
iters, ≤8 decode tokens); no memory sweep.

## Handoff

- **Batty:** Metal prefill now beats CPU at 256/512 tokens — **re-run `hybrid_prefill_decode`**; the
  GPU-prefill/CPU-decode hybrid should now win total latency in that range (KV-handoff already
  proven + free).
- **Next Metal levers (not MatMulNBits):** the 1024-token residual deficit is the **O(M²) GQA
  attention** kernel (prefill flash-attention tiling) and the double-buffered command-buffer submit
  — profile with `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_PROFILE`.

## Files changed (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)

- `src/kernels/matmulnbits.metal` — **new** `mps_matmulnbits_gemm_f32` (simdgroup_matrix MMA tiled
  prefill GEMM; fp16 tiles, fp32 accumulate; dequant-in-threadgroup).
- `src/ep/metal_context.mm` — register the pipeline; `Auto` M≥threshold → GemmF32 dispatch
  (threadgroups); `_GEMM_THRESHOLD` / `_NOGEMM` env knobs.
- `src/ep/metal_context.h` — `GemmF32` enum value + doc.
- `src/ep/ep.cc` — comment: prefill auto-routes on runtime M (no behavior change).
- `tests/kernels/mariette_kernel_test.cc` — new `TestMatMulNBitsGemm` (M=8/32/128 + ragged M/N vs
  fp32 ref).
- `tests/e2e/e2e_test.cc` — `MeasurePrefill` (warmup + best-of-N prefill/TTFT), `[pad_to]` +
  `[prefill_iters]` args (token-repeat padding for the length sweep), prefill/TTFT report line.

## Reproduce

```bash
cd onnxruntime-mps && cmake --build build -j8
ORTLIB=$(dirname $(ls <onnx-genai>/target/release/build/onnx-genai-ort-sys-*/out/ort-prebuilt/lib/libonnxruntime.dylib|head -1))
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ctest --test-dir build --output-on-failure           # 3/3 PASS
MODEL=<onnx-genai>/models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx
EP=$PWD/build/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib
# coherence gate (unpadded 26-tok):
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./build/mps_e2e "$MODEL" "$EP" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8         # PASS
# TTFT sweep: mps_e2e <model> <ep> <tokens> <num_new> <pad_to> <prefill_iters>
for P in 0 256 512 1024; do
  DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ./build/mps_e2e "$MODEL" "$EP" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8 $P 4 | grep prefill/TTFT
done
# A/B vs old GEMV-replicated prefill:
DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=$ORTLIB ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_NOGEMM=1 ./build/mps_e2e "$MODEL" "$EP" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8 512 4
```
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-batched-dispatch.md -->
### 2026-07-13: Metal EP batched dispatch — subgraph fusion + one-command-buffer-per-subgraph + device-resident intermediates

**By:** Nabil (ORT Plugin EP Engineer)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` (builds `libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib`)
**What:** Rebuilt the Metal EP's *dispatch architecture* (partitioning + command-buffer +
intermediate memory). Kernel math is unchanged (Mariette/Coco own it). This removes the
per-node `commit`+`waitUntilCompleted`+host-memcpy round-trips that were masking the
MatMulNBits win.

---

#### The problem (measured, before)
- 366/393 nodes ran on the Apple GPU, output bit-identical to CPU, but decode was
  **11.3–11.7 tok/s Metal vs ~155–170 CPU** — a *loss*.
- Root cause was architectural, not the kernels: the EP claimed/executed each supported node
  as **its own tiny fused node**, and every node dispatch did `commit` + `waitUntilCompleted`
  + host copy-back ⇒ **~366 GPU submissions and 366 CPU↔GPU syncs per token**.

#### The fix (3 parts)

**1. Fuse maximal *convex* connected subgraphs in `GetCapability`.**
Instead of one fused node per supported node, we group supported nodes into maximal, connected,
**convex** clusters and `EpGraphSupportInfo_AddNodesToFuse` **once per cluster**. ORT then hands
the whole subgraph to a single `Compile`/Compute.
- *Convexity is mandatory.* A residual edge skips each GroupQueryAttention (pre-attn SkipLN
  `out[3]` → post-attn SkipLN input) while another path goes pre-attn → GQA → post-attn. GQA is
  **not** claimed (falls to CPU), so it is an *external* node lying on a path between two
  cluster members. Contracting a cluster that straddles it creates a cycle ⇒ ORT rejects the
  fusion. We enforce convexity with a reachability-bitset test: a candidate set `S` is convex
  iff no node `x ∉ S` has (`S` reaches `x`) AND (`x` reaches `S`). Implemented in
  `BuildConvexClusters` (Kahn topo-order → per-node reachability bitsets → union-find merge of
  supported↔supported data edges, accepting a merge only if the union stays convex).
- Result on Qwen2.5-0.5B: **366 nodes → 27 fused subgraphs** (the 24 CPU-side GQA nodes are the
  dominant splitters). `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_NOFUSE=1` disables clustering (each supported node
  its own singleton, still via the new executor) for isolating executor vs clustering bugs.

**2. One `MTLCommandBuffer` per subgraph execution.**
`MetalContext` gained a batch scope: `BeginBatch()` opens ONE command buffer + ONE **serial**
`MTLComputeCommandEncoder`; every kernel dispatch encodes into it; `EndBatch()` does the single
`commit` + `waitUntilCompleted` and runs any deferred host copy-backs. The default
(parameterless) compute encoder is `MTLDispatchTypeSerial`, so data-dependent dispatches in the
buffer observe each other's writes with **no explicit barriers**. The subgraph executor
(`RunSubgraph`) wraps all nodes of a fused subgraph in exactly one `BeginBatch`/`EndBatch`.
- Measured: **216 total GPU commits for 8 graph passes = exactly 27 commits/token**
  (one per fused subgraph), down from ~366/token — a **~13.5× reduction in GPU round-trips**.
  (Instrument: `MetalContext::CommitCount()` + `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_PROFILE=1` prints the
  session total from the `MetalContext` dtor.)

**3. Device-resident intermediates (no host memcpy between kernels).**
Subgraph-internal edges are allocated as unified-memory `MTLBuffer`s from the existing
allocator and threaded kernel→kernel. Only subgraph **inputs** (from ORT/CPU) and **outputs**
(to ORT / the next CPU-fallback op) cross the host boundary. A per-plan pool
(`SubgraphPlan::AcquireIntermediate`) reuses each intermediate buffer across decode steps when
the shape is stable (frees only on shape growth / plan teardown). MatMulNBits' constant int4
weights + scales are still copied to device buffers once on first Compute and reused (unchanged
from Mariette's kernel).

**4. Unified kernel interface (`KernelIO` / `KernelBase::ComputeIO`).**
Kernels no longer talk to `OrtKernelContext` directly; they take a `KernelIO` (yielding
`IOTensor{data, mutable_data, shape, type, element_count}`) and encode into whatever command
buffer is currently open. `SubgraphIO` implements `KernelIO` over the fused subgraph: input
resolution priority is **Intermediate (produced in-subgraph) → CtxInput (fused-node input) →
Initializer (`graph.GetInitializers()`)**; outputs are ORT `ctx.GetOutput` for subgraph outputs
or pooled device buffers for intermediates. Mariette's and Coco's kernels are unified onto this
one interface — **their math is byte-for-byte unchanged**, only the I/O glue moved.

---

#### Results (Qwen2.5-0.5B, M1 Max, 8-token E2E, `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH` = prebuilt ORT 1.27)
| metric | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| fused subgraphs (claim units) | ~366 (1/node) | **27** |
| GPU commits / decode token | ~366 | **27** (measured 216/8) |
| decode tok/s (Metal) | 11.3–11.7 | **~66 steady** (first-token warmup drags the 7-token avg; cold runs ~26–52) |
| decode tok/s (CPU ref, same run) | ~155–170 | ~150–170 |
| coherence vs CPU (8 tok) | PASS | **PASS (bit-identical)** |
| all 3 ctest suites | pass | **pass** |

**~5.5× faster decode, ~13.5× fewer host round-trips, coherence preserved.** Metal is no longer
a loss but has not yet caught CPU.

#### Where the remaining time goes (scope for next round)
1. **GroupQueryAttention runs on CPU** (24 nodes, not claimed). This is what shatters the decoder
   into 27 subgraphs and forces 27 commit+wait syncs and host boundary copies per token. Putting
   a Metal GQA/attention kernel on the EP would collapse 27→~1–2 subgraphs and roughly eliminate
   the per-layer CPU↔GPU stalls — the single biggest remaining lever.
2. **27 serial commit+wait sync points/token.** Each `EndBatch` blocks the CPU on the GPU. With
   GQA on-device this largely disappears; short of that, overlapping encode of subgraph N+1 with
   GPU execution of N (or a shared timeline/event instead of `waitUntilCompleted` at internal
   boundaries) would help.
3. **MatMulNBits kernel micro-opt** (169 nodes dominate GPU compute): the int8
   `accuracy_level=4` path for MatMulNBits is the intended next kernel change (Mariette). Dispatch
   is no longer the bottleneck, so kernel gains will now show up in tok/s.
   A finer *per-op GPU* profile needs command-buffer timestamp sampling (all nodes share one
   buffer) — deferred to that round.

#### Files changed (no commit — coordinator commits)
- `src/ep/ep.h` — new `IOTensor` / `KernelIO` / `KernelBase::ComputeIO` interface; kernels
  inherit `KernelBase`; `MetalEp` owns `plans_` (fused-name → `SubgraphPlan`).
- `src/ep/ep.cc` — kernels transformed to `ComputeIO` (math unchanged); `AddClaimable` /
  `NodeClaimable`; `BuildConvexClusters`; `SubgraphPlan`/`NodePlan`/`InputRef`/`OutputRef`,
  `SubgraphIO`, `RunSubgraph`; rewritten `GetCapability` (cluster + fuse, log cluster count,
  honor `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_NOFUSE`) and `Compile` (build per-subgraph plan, topo-sort).
- `src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}` — `BeginBatch`/`EndBatch`/`BatchActive`; batch-aware `Dispatch`
  and `RunPass` (encode into shared serial encoder, defer copy-backs + owned-temp release to
  `EndBatch`, also fixes a pre-existing wrapped-temp leak); `CommitCount()` +
  `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_PROFILE` commit-count print.
- `src/ep/ep_factory.cc` — `config.claim_mariette = enabled` under `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_CLAIM`.

#### Notes / gotchas for the team
- **MRR, not ARC** (no `-fobjc-arc`): `[queue commandBuffer]` / `[cmd computeCommandEncoder]` are
  autoreleased (+0) — `retain` in `BeginBatch`, `release` in `EndBatch`, or they die with the
  per-dispatch autorelease pool.
- **8-token coherence is the bar.** At ≥~14 tokens the stream diverges from CPU due to a
  pre-existing fp32 reduction-order drift in the kernels (not dispatch) — out of scope here.
- Serial encoder ordering is what makes single-buffer multi-dispatch correct; if a future kernel
  needs it, the fallback is `memoryBarrierWithScope:MTLBarrierScopeBuffers` between dispatches.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-ep-skeleton.md -->
### 2026-07-12: Metal/MPS ONNX Runtime plugin-EP — Phase-1 skeleton as-built (ABI contract, device enumeration, partition/fallback, kernel interface)

**By:** Nabil
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` (builds `libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib`)
**What:** Implemented and proved the Phase-1 EP skeleton from Roy's design (`docs/DESIGN.md`).
A stock **prebuilt ORT 1.27.0** (`ORT_API_VERSION 27`, the `onnxruntime-osx-arm64-1.27.0` that
`onnx-genai/ort-sys` downloads) loads our `.dylib`, partitions the real Qwen2.5-0.5B graph,
runs it with CPU fallback, and produces coherent output. **No ORT fork, no ORT rebuild.**
Verified end-to-end: prompt "What is the capital of France?" ⇒ **"The capital of France is
Paris."**, and the MetalEP token stream is **bit-identical** to a pure-CPU session (coherence
gate PASS) in both pure-fallback and Add-on-Metal modes.

---

#### 1. Plugin-EP entry-point contract (as-built)

- The `.dylib` exports exactly **two** C symbols (`__attribute__((visibility("default")))`,
  everything else hidden via `-fvisibility=hidden`):
  - `OrtStatus* CreateEpFactories(const char* registration_name, const OrtApiBase* ort_api_base, OrtEpFactory** factories, size_t max_factories, size_t* num_factories)`
  - `OrtStatus* ReleaseEpFactory(OrtEpFactory* factory)`
- `CreateEpFactories` does: `ort_api_base->GetApi(ORT_API_VERSION)` → `Ort::InitApi(ort_api)`
  (header-only C++ API is compiled with `ORT_API_MANUAL_INIT`) → `ep_api = ort_api->GetEpApi()`
  → construct one `MetalEpFactory`.
- **ABI SURPRISE / R2 RESOLVED:** the prebuilt dylib exports **only** `_OrtGetApiBase`
  (plus the CPU/CoreML provider-factory C funcs). `RegisterExecutionProviderLibrary`,
  `GetEpApi`, `SessionOptionsAppendExecutionProvider_V2`, `CreateEpFactories` etc. are **not**
  standalone dynamic symbols — they are **members of the `OrtApi` / `OrtEpApi` function-pointer
  structs** reached via `OrtGetApiBase()->GetApi(27)`. The design's suggested
  `nm libonnxruntime | grep ExecutionProviderLibrary` check is therefore **misleading and will
  always come up empty even on a full (non-minimal) build.** The correct capability proof is
  compiling against `onnxruntime_ep_c_api.h` and successfully calling
  `RegisterExecutionProviderLibrary` at runtime (which we now do).
- **Header gotcha:** `onnxruntime_ep_c_api.h` has **no include guard / `#pragma once`** and is
  already `#include`d transitively at the bottom of `onnxruntime_c_api.h`. Including it a second
  time yourself causes redefinition errors (`OrtEp`, `OrtEpFactory`, …). Include only
  `onnxruntime_cxx_api.h` (or `onnxruntime_c_api.h`) and let it pull the EP header in once.
- Struct init discipline: value-initialize the ABI base (`OrtEp{}`, `OrtEpFactory{}`,
  `OrtNodeComputeInfo{}`) to null every optional/newer field, set
  `ort_version_supported = ORT_API_VERSION`, then assign only the function pointers we
  implement. Implemented `OrtEpFactory`: `GetName`, `GetVendor`, `GetVendorId`, `GetVersion`,
  `GetSupportedDevices`, `CreateEp`, `ReleaseEp`, `CreateAllocator`/`ReleaseAllocator`,
  `CreateDataTransfer`, `IsStreamAware=false`. Implemented `OrtEp`: `GetName`, `GetCapability`,
  `Compile`, `ReleaseNodeComputeInfos`, `GetDefaultMemoryDevice`. Implemented
  `OrtNodeComputeInfo`: `CreateState`, `Compute`, `ReleaseState`.

#### 2. Device enumeration (design open question O3 / #4) — RESOLVED

- **Apple GPU enumerates automatically. `CreateHardwareDevice` is NOT needed.** ORT's built-in
  device discovery surfaces the Apple silicon as `OrtHardwareDevice` instances and passes the
  full list into `OrtEpFactory::GetSupportedDevices`. On this M1 Max, ORT enumerates **three**
  Apple devices (all `vendor_id=0x106B`): `type=2` (NPU/ANE), `type=1` (**GPU**), `type=0`
  (CPU) — where `OrtHardwareDeviceType` is `CPU=0, GPU=1, NPU=2`.
- **As-built policy:** `GetSupportedDevices` logs every enumerated hw device, then binds MetalEP
  to the **GPU** hw device if present (else falls back to CPU), calls
  `EpDevice_AddAllocatorInfo` with our unified-memory GPU `OrtMemoryInfo`, and
  `CreateEpDevice`. We advertise a GPU allocator while binding to the ORT-surfaced GPU
  `OrtHardwareDevice` — we do **not** synthesize one.

#### 3. Partition + fallback approach (as-built)

- **Compile-based path** (matches ORT's canonical `example_plugin_ep`), simplest correct
  Phase-1 choice; the kernel-registry path is deferred to Phase 2.
- `GetCapability` walks the graph and claims nodes via
  `EpGraphSupportInfo_AddNodesToFuse(one_node, 1, &fusion_opts)` — **one node per fusion group**
  so ORT compiles each claimed node independently. Everything not claimed **falls back to the
  ORT CPU EP automatically**.
- **Phase-1 claim filter (coherence-safe):** standard `ai.onnx` **`Add`** only, float32 in/out,
  where the two inputs are either **equal shape** or a **trailing-suffix broadcast** (e.g. a
  bias add `[batch, seq, C] + [C]`). Shapes are compared **symbolically** (concrete dims must be
  equal; symbolic dims must share the same name) so it works despite dynamic `batch`/`seq`.
  Interior/general broadcasting is rejected.
- On the target model (`qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe`, 393 nodes after ORT AOT inlining): all
  activation+activation residual Adds are already fused away; the **72 remaining `Add` nodes are
  bias adds** `[batch, seq, C] + [C]`. With the default config MetalEP claims **72/393** and runs
  them on the GPU; with `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_CLAIM=none` it claims **0/393** (pure fallback).
  Both produce identical, coherent output.
- `Compile` builds one `AddKernel` + `OrtNodeComputeInfo{CreateState,Compute,ReleaseState}` per
  fused node. `NodeComputeContext_NodeName` keys the kernel lookup at `CreateState`.
- **Env switch:** `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_CLAIM=none` disables Add-claiming (pure CPU fallback),
  useful as a guaranteed-coherent smoke test before enabling Metal ops.

#### 4. Metal execution / memory (as-built)

- `MetalContext` (Obj-C++ `.mm`) owns `MTLDevice` (`MTLCreateSystemDefaultDevice`), a command
  queue, and the kernel library. **`metallib` is NOT installed on this machine**, so the shader
  is compiled **at runtime** from an embedded source string via
  `newLibraryWithSource:options:error:`. `src/kernels/elementwise.metal` is the canonical
  reviewable copy; switch to a precompiled `default.metallib` when the kernel set grows.
- Allocator = shared-storage (`MTLResourceStorageModeShared`) `MTLBuffer` pool; `Alloc` returns
  `buffer.contents` (CPU-addressable, unified memory) and a `contents-ptr → MTLBuffer` map lets
  kernels dispatch against device buffers with no copy. `OrtDataTransferImpl` is a `memcpy`
  (unified memory ⇒ no host↔device round-trip). Foreign host pointers (e.g. ORT-owned CPU
  tensors / initializers) are wrapped in a temporary shared buffer for the dispatch.

#### 5. Kernel interface for Mariette/Coco (implement against this)

Add kernels to `src/kernels/*.metal` (same runtime-compiled path) following this dispatch
contract, which the EP layer guarantees:

- Tensors are **contiguous, row-major, fp32** in Phase 1 (fp16 variants Phase 2).
- **`buffer(0..N-1)` = the ordered ONNX node inputs; `buffer(N)` = the output.**
- After the buffers come `constant uint&` scalars: **one element-count per operand, then the
  output element count last.** For a 2-input op: `buffer(3)=na`, `buffer(4)=nb`, `buffer(5)=n`.
- EP dispatches `dispatchThreads` over a **1-D grid of `n` (one thread per output element)**.
- Broadcasting is limited to equal shapes or trailing-suffix (bias) broadcast; the EP guarantees
  each operand's element count evenly divides `n` and maps to the trailing dims, so the kernel
  uses `idx % n_operand` to index. General/interior broadcasting (shape+stride uniforms) is a
  Phase-2 addition.
- Reference kernel shipped: `mps_add_f32(a, b, c, na, nb, n)` →
  `c[i] = a[i % na] + b[i % nb]`. TODO for kernel engineers:
  `mps_mul_f32`, `mps_sub_f32`, `mps_sigmoid_f32`, `mps_silu_f32` (Sigmoid∘Mul), `mps_cast_*`,
  then fp16 + `RMSNormalization`/`SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization` per the design's P1/P2.

#### 6. Build + test (verified commands)

```
# configure + build (system Xcode clang; auto-detects onnx-genai's cached ORT prebuilt)
cmake -S . -B build -G "Unix Makefiles"
cmake --build build -j4        # -> build/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib (+ mps_e2e)

# e2e (prompt fixture generated by tests/e2e/prepare_prompt.py)
export DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=<onnx-genai ort-prebuilt>/lib
./build/mps_e2e <model.onnx> $PWD/build/libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt 8
#   default  -> claimed 72/393, COHERENCE PASS
#   ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_CLAIM=none -> claimed 0/393 (pure fallback), COHERENCE PASS
```

**Result:** coherent output in both modes; MetalEP token stream == CPU token stream.
Baseline decode tok/s on this M1 Max (0.5B, fp32, short greedy): pure-fallback ≈ **120–150
tok/s** (parity with CPU-only, as expected — same CPU kernels); Add-on-Metal ≈ **39 tok/s**
(slower because each of the 72 bias-Adds does a synchronous `waitUntilCompleted` + host-buffer
wrap per decode step — a **correctness** milestone, not a perf one; batching/async + keeping KV
and activations resident in `MTLBuffer` is the Phase-2 perf work).

**Files created (none committed — coordinator commits):**
`CMakeLists.txt`, `cmake/FindONNXRuntime.cmake`, `include/onnxruntime_mps/{version.h,metal_ep.h}`,
`src/ep/{plugin_ep_utils.h,metal_context.h,metal_context.mm,allocator.h,data_transfer.h,
data_transfer.cc,ep.h,ep.cc,ep_factory.h,ep_factory.cc,entry.cc}`,
`src/kernels/elementwise.metal`, `tests/e2e/{e2e_test.cc,prepare_prompt.py}`, `.gitignore`
(adds `build/`, `.ref/`, `tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt`).

**Impact / hand-off:** Mariette/Coco implement kernels against §5. The onnx-genai
`ONNX_GENAI_EP=metal` wiring (separate task) registers this `.dylib` via
`RegisterExecutionProviderLibrary` + `SessionOptionsAppendExecutionProvider_V2` selecting EP
name **"MetalEP"** (vendor Apple, `vendor_id=0x106B`).


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-mlx-claim-attrs.md -->
# Decision: MLX EP Claimable-in-registry + generic NodeDesc attributes (zero-ep.cc-edit op add)

**Author:** Nabil (EP architect)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mlx` (HEAD was af35291; NOT committed — coordinator commits after validating green)
**Scope:** Completes the modularization begun in `nabil-mlx-op-registry`. Makes adding ANY op **purely
additive** (one self-contained handler module + one registration line, **no `ep.cc` edits**). This
unblocks parallel op-coverage work (Slice/Reduce\*/Conv/Attention/Split/LayerNorm with array & string
attrs).

---

## Summary

Two changes, both pure refactor (claim set + numerics byte-identical):

1. **Claim moved into the registry.** Each `OpRegistration` now carries a `ClaimPredicate` next to its
   translate handler. The per-op dtype/shape/attribute claim logic that lived in `ep.cc`
   (`AddClaimable`/`MarietteClaimable`/`CocoClaimable`) moved into the corresponding `ops/*.cc`
   modules. `ep.cc` now calls a single generic hook `ort_mps_mlx::Claimable(node)`.
2. **Generic attribute extraction.** `ep.cc` copies **all** of a node's ONNX attributes into
   `NodeDesc` generically (replacing the fixed 10-scalar list), and `NodeDesc` gained **int-array,
   float-array, and string** maps (in addition to scalar int/float) so the long-tail ops
   (Slice/Reduce/Transpose/Conv/Split/LayerNorm) can be represented at all.

The `claim_add`/`claim_coco`/`claim_mariette` family flags collapsed to a single
`Config::claim_enabled` kill-switch (`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_CLAIM=none` still claims nothing).

---

## 1. Claimable-in-registry API

```cpp
using OpHandler     = void (*)(TranslationContext& ctx, const NodeDesc& node);
using ClaimPredicate = bool (*)(Ort::ConstNode node);

struct OpRegistration {
  std::string domain;
  std::string op_type;
  int min_opset = kAnyOpset;
  int max_opset = kAnyOpset;
  OpHandler       handler   = nullptr;   // run-time translate
  ClaimPredicate  claimable = nullptr;   // claim-time dtype/shape/attr checks
};

// Registration = translate + claim, together, in the op module:
registry.Register({domain, "MyOp", min_opset, max_opset, &MyOp, &MyOpClaim});
```

- `OpRegistry::FindEntry(domain, op, since_version)` returns the whole matching entry (opset-range
  aware); `Find()` is now `FindEntry()->handler`.
- **`ep.cc` claim hook** (all per-op logic gone from `ep.cc`):

  ```cpp
  bool Claimable(Ort::ConstNode node) {   // in op_registry.cc, declared in mlx_backend.h
    const OpRegistration* e = OpRegistry::Instance().FindEntry(
        node.GetDomain(), node.GetOperatorType(), node.GetSinceVersion());
    return e && e->claimable && e->claimable(node);
  }
  // ep.cc GetCapability: supported[i] = config.claim_enabled && ort_mps_mlx::Claimable(nodes[i]);
  ```

  This folds the old `Supported()` membership AND-gate into the same lookup — a claimed op is always
  translatable.
- **Shared claim helpers** live in the new reusable header `src/ep/op_claim.h`: `TensorInfo`,
  `IsMlxFloatType` (fp32/fp16/bf16), `IsFloatType` (fp32/fp16), `IntAttribute`, `FloatAttribute`,
  `SuffixBroadcast` (strict, fp32 residual add), `ScalarOrSuffixBroadcast` (lenient, fp16/bf16/int).
  The claim predicates in `ops/*.cc` are thin wrappers over these.
- The claim predicates read materialized-default attributes straight off the node via
  `IntAttribute`/`FloatAttribute` (unchanged semantics), e.g. GQA's `smooth_softmax == -1` default.

## 2. Generic `NodeDesc` attribute schema

`ep.cc`'s node-build loop iterates `node.GetAttributes()` and dispatches on `ConstOpAttr::GetType()`:

| ONNX attr type      | `NodeDesc` map                                   | Handler read example        |
|---------------------|--------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------|
| `ORT_OP_ATTR_INT`   | `ints`        `map<string,int64_t>`              | `n.ints.at("axis")`         |
| `ORT_OP_ATTR_FLOAT` | `floats`      `map<string,float>`                | `n.floats.at("epsilon")`    |
| `ORT_OP_ATTR_INTS`  | `int_arrays`  `map<string,vector<int64_t>>`      | `n.int_arrays.at("axes")`   |
| `ORT_OP_ATTR_FLOATS`| `float_arrays``map<string,vector<float>>`        | `n.float_arrays.at("scales")`|
| `ORT_OP_ATTR_STRING`| `strings`     `map<string,string>`               | `n.strings.at("mode")`      |

- Absent attrs are simply not in the map → optional read is
  `n.<map>.count(k) ? n.<map>.at(k) : <default>`; required read is `n.<map>.at(k)`.
- `STRINGS`/`GRAPH`/`TENSOR` are **not** carried yet (no claimed op reads them). Adding one is a
  localized `ep.cc` + `NodeDesc` change — still no per-op branching.
- **Identical-behavior guarantee:** the migrated handlers' fallback defaults already matched the old
  `ep.cc` `IntAttribute/FloatAttribute` defaults (e.g. `epsilon` → `1e-6f`, GBQ `block_size` → `32`),
  so dropping the fixed list changes no numerics. Required attrs (`K`, `N`, `num_heads`,
  `kv_num_heads`) are real node attributes, always present.

## 3. Final ZERO-`ep.cc`-edit add-an-op recipe

1. In `src/ep/ops/<family>.cc`: add `void MyOp(TranslationContext&, const NodeDesc&)` (translate) and
   `bool MyOpClaim(Ort::ConstNode)` (claim, using `op_claim.h` helpers). Read attrs generically
   (`n.ints`/`n.int_arrays`/`n.strings`/…).
2. One line: `registry.Register({domain, "MyOp", min, max, &MyOp, &MyOpClaim});`.
3. New module only: add the `.cc` to `CMakeLists.txt`, declare `RegisterMyFamilyOps` in
   `op_registry.h`, call it in `op_registry.cc::RegisterBuiltinOps`.
4. Add pytest cases in `tests/ops/` (IR-API via `_models.py`).

**No edits to `ep.cc`, `mlx_backend.cc`, or the engine.** (Only a new `STRINGS`/`GRAPH`/`TENSOR` attr
type would touch `ep.cc`/`NodeDesc` — no current op needs it.)

---

## Verification (all green)

- Build: `cmake --build build -j8` green (mlx-c hard dep).
- `ctest --test-dir build`: 3/3 — `mlx_op_tests` (pytest **31 passed**), `mlx_leak_test` (flat),
  `mlx_e2e`.
- E2E coherence **byte-identical**: tokens `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645`
  ("The capital of France is Paris"), COHERENCE GATE PASS.
- Claimed-node count **unchanged**: **390 of 393 nodes across 1 fused subgraph** (decoder still fuses
  to one subgraph) — confirms the claim set is identical.
- MRR `[release]` memory safety preserved (no allocator/cache-limit changes).

## Files touched

- `src/ep/op_registry.{h,cc}` — `ClaimPredicate`, `OpRegistration::claimable`, `FindEntry`,
  `Claimable(node)`.
- `src/ep/mlx_backend.h` — `NodeDesc` int/float **arrays** + `strings` maps; `Claimable` decl.
- `src/ep/op_claim.h` — **new** shared claim helpers.
- `src/ep/ops/{elementwise,norm,attention,quant}.cc` — per-op claim predicates + 2-arg registration.
- `src/ep/ep.cc` — deleted `*Claimable` + helpers; thin `NodeClaimable`→`Claimable`; generic attr copy.
- `src/ep/ep.h`, `src/ep/ep_factory.cc` — `claim_*` family flags → single `claim_enabled`.
- `docs/OP_ARCHITECTURE.md` — §0/§2.2/§5/§6 updated (registry-owns-claim, generic attrs, zero-ep.cc recipe).


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-mlx-evaluation.md -->
# MLX (mlx-c) as a Metal EP compute backend — evaluation SPIKE (measured on M1 Max)

**By:** Nabil (ORT Plugin EP Engineer — architecture owner, Metal EP)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, 32 GB UMA (same box as the perf in this decisions log)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` · **Requested by:** Justin Chu
**No commit — coordinator commits.** Prototype stays isolated in `spike/mlx/` (not in the main build).

## TL;DR

Ran a rigorous, **measured** spike on Justin's question — *"能不能用 mlx 的 c api，比 mps 更快，省点
事情?"* MLX's int4 kernels **are** faster than ours on our exact weights/shapes/machine
(**decode ~1.5–1.8×, prefill ~2.5–3×**), our ONNX MatMulNBits format **maps to MLX losslessly**, and
MLX's C API covers **most of the ~77 ops we'd otherwise hand-write**. **The catch:** mlx-c exposes
**no way to share our `MTLBuffer` pool zero-copy** (metal.h = capture only; no MTLBuffer wrap / no
allocator hook). So the win only lands if we hand MLX **whole fused subgraphs** — op-by-op or
fine-grained hybrid pays a copy+`eval`-sync tax that can erase it. **Recommendation: (C) coarse
hybrid, gated behind an E2E prototype — do NOT rip out our kernels, do NOT add a hard MLX build dep
yet.** Full writeup: [`docs/MLX_EVALUATION.md`](../../../../onnxruntime-mps/docs/MLX_EVALUATION.md).

## What was measured (not asserted)

Isolated prototype `spike/mlx/` — `mlx_probe.c` (API/interop/format) + `bench_matmulnbits.mm` (our
`mps_matmulnbits_f32_v` GEMV + `mps_matmulnbits_gemm_f32` GEMM **vs** `mlx_quantized_matmul` on one
logical int4/block-32/zp-8 weight expressed in both layouts). mlx 0.30.5 / mlx-c 0.5.0 (Homebrew).
Reproduce: `cmake -S spike/mlx -B spike/mlx/build && cmake --build spike/mlx/build`.

**1. C API surface + maturity — HIGH.** `mlx_quantized_matmul` (affine int4/int8, group∈{32,64,128}),
`mlx_gather_qmm` (MoE), `mlx_fast_scaled_dot_product_attention` (GQA + causal/array mask + sinks),
`mlx_fast_rope`, `mlx_fast_rms_norm`/`layer_norm`, full elementwise/reduce/gather/conv, `mlx_eval`/
`mlx_synchronize`/streams, `mlx_set_cache_limit`/`wired_limit`, and `mlx_fast_metal_kernel` as a
custom-MSL escape hatch. Covers the whole decode + MoE path.

**2. Zero-copy interop — the decisive finding (measured).**
- `mlx_array_new_data` → **COPIES** (probe: different pointer).
- `mlx_array_new_data_managed(ptr,…,dtor)` → **adopts in place** (probe: **same address**), zero-copy —
  but only for **page-aligned whole buffers** (sub-tensor offsets into our pool are unaligned → copy),
  and with an ownership/`dtor` contract that fights our MRR pool.
- `metal.h` has **no** MTLBuffer-wrap, **no** allocator injection, **no** MTLBuffer extraction.
- ⇒ **True bidirectional pool sharing is not possible via the public C API.** Best case: adopt whole
  page-aligned inputs, copy/hand-off on output. This caps the integration ceiling.

**3. Quant format maps cleanly (measured).** ONNX `(q-8)·scale` = MLX affine `q·scale + bias` with
**`bias = -8·scale`, group_size=32**. Probe vs CPU ref: **max abs err 2.3e-8**. Repack uint8→uint32 is
a **one-time, lossless** reshuffle at `Compile` (O(weights), <1 s for 0.5B), not a per-token cost.

**4. Perf (M1 Max; lm_head 68 MB > SLC = trustworthy true-DRAM number):**

| Regime | Ours | MLX | MLX edge |
|---|---|---|---|
| Decode matmul M=1, DRAM-bound | ~95–123 GB/s | **~185–199 GB/s** | **~1.5–1.8×** |
| Prefill matmul M=256 | ~2.2–2.5 TFLOP/s | **~5.1–6.8 TFLOP/s** | **~2.5–3×** |

**Honesty caveat:** the naive `GB/s ÷ 0.35` tok/s-equiv (ours≈300 / MLX≈560) is an **upper bound**.
Real decode = 169 *small, data-dependent* matmuls + attn + norms fused per token (why we're at 46 GB/s
/ 133 tok/s, not the single-op peak). MLX would also fall from its peak there. Realistic expectation:
kernel edge lifts decode from ~133 toward **~180–220 tok/s** (not 560); prefill gains more. Confirming
needs an E2E prototype — out of spike scope.

**5. EP integration.** ORT's sync `Compute` can drive MLX's lazy graph via one `mlx_eval` at the end
(replaces our `BeginBatch/EndBatch`). Good fit because **post-GQA the entire decoder is already ONE
fused subgraph** — an ideal hand-off unit. Risks: (a) no pool sharing ⇒ boundary copies; (b) **hybrid
ping-pong tax** — ours↔MLX handoffs each cost `eval`+copy, so hybrid only pays at **coarse**
granularity (whole attention/MLP blocks, glue stays ours); (c) dual allocators vs the MRR-leak history
(bound with `mlx_set_cache_limit`); (d) new hard dep (`libmlxc`+`libmlx`); (e) quant lock-in + accuracy
gating on MLX upgrades.

## Decision / recommendation

**Adopt (C) a bounded, coarse-grained hybrid — but gate it behind a Phase-0 E2E prototype; keep our
kernels; no hard MLX build dep until the gate passes.** The kernel speedups are real and the effort
saved on the ~77-op long tail is the bigger prize, but the interop ceiling means MLX must own *whole*
subgraphs. Fits the op-registry: a `KernelFactory` can build an "MLX subgraph" handler per
(domain,op,opset) **at coarse granularity**.

**Phased plan:** (0) `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_USE_MLX` flag routes the whole fused decoder subgraph through
MLX (qmm+SDPA+rope+rms_norm), repack weights at `Compile`; measure real Qwen2.5-0.5B tok/s + 8-token
coherence gate — **proceed only if E2E ≥ ~1.25× and accuracy holds.** (1) bound interop (page-aligned
pool for `managed` adopt; single copy-out; leak-test vs `mps_leak_test`). (2) coarse MLX-backed
`OpHandler`s (MatMulNBits/GQA/prefill) selectable per node, native default+fallback. (3) long-tail
coverage via MLX primitives + `mlx_fast_metal_kernel`, retiring the hand-write-77 burden. **If Phase 0
fails**, fall back to (A) for decode and keep MLX only as a candidate for **prefill** (largest, coarse,
compute-bound win).

## Verification / hygiene

- Main build **green**, `ctest` **4/4 pass** incl. `mps_leak_test` — spike touches nothing shipping
  (main `CMakeLists.txt` lists sources explicitly; `spike/` is unreferenced).
- Bounded runs only; no MRR leak reintroduced (prototype frees all MLX arrays/streams; MLX cache
  bounded conceptually for any real integration).

## Files created (all in `onnxruntime-mps`, uncommitted)

- `docs/MLX_EVALUATION.md` — full findings + A/B/C recommendation + phased plan.
- `spike/mlx/mlx_probe.c` — version/backend, zero-copy interop probe, quant-format correctness.
- `spike/mlx/bench_matmulnbits.mm` — our-kernels-vs-MLX decode/prefill benchmark.
- `spike/mlx/CMakeLists.txt` — **isolated** build target (not referenced by main build).
- `spike/mlx/README.md`, `spike/mlx/.gitignore`.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-mlx-full-pivot.md -->
# Full MLX pivot — MLX is the SOLE compute path, hand kernels DELETED, repo is `onnxruntime-mlx`

**By:** Nabil (ORT Plugin EP Engineer — architecture owner)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, 32 GB UMA
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mlx` (renamed from `onnxruntime-mps`) · HEAD was clean at 5d64699
**Model:** `qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx` (int4 block-32, fp32 activations)
**Requested by:** Justin Chu — DECISION IS FINAL ("不用再研究了 全面改mlx" / "保留单个高效的impl")
**No commit — coordinator commits after validating green.**

## TL;DR
Executed the full pivot. The whole fused decoder subgraph (both **prefill AND decode**) now runs
through **MLX as the one and only compute path**. All hand-written Metal (`.metal`) kernels and their
machinery are **deleted**. `mlx-c` is a **hard build dependency**. Build green, `ctest` 3/3 green,
E2E coherent ("The capital of France is Paris"), memory perfectly flat. Prefill 2.2×–3.5× vs CPU;
decode warm ~122–148 tok/s (consistent with Phase-0's 1.02–1.09× vs the now-removed hand path).

This promotes Phase-0's full-MLX path to default. Phase-0 had recommended a *hybrid* (MLX prefill +
hand-kernel decode) because full-MLX decode was ~parity (not a ≥1.25× win). The user overrode that on
the "one efficient impl" principle: MLX is **Pareto-dominant** (never slower on decode + big prefill
win + coherent + memory-stable), so we keep ONE implementation (MLX) and drop the hand kernels.

## Compatibility — action for `onnx-genai` (IMPORTANT)
- **Registered EP name is UNCHANGED: `"MetalEP"`.** The factory uses the `registration_name` passed
  to `CreateEpFactories` (onnx-genai passes `REGISTRATION_NAME = "MetalEP"`), so `GetName` /
  `EpDevice_EpName` still report `"MetalEP"`. **No change needed to `session.rs` (~line 871).**
- **New dylib name: `libonnxruntime_mlx_ep.dylib`** (was `libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib`).
- onnx-genai locates the plugin via the `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_LIB` env var (absolute path, no
  hardcoded filename). **Only action:** point `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_LIB` at the new
  `libonnxruntime_mlx_ep.dylib`. Zero Rust code changes.
- Vendor string changed `onnxruntime-mps` → `onnxruntime-mlx`; vendor id unchanged (`0x106B`).

## What was DELETED
- `src/kernels/*.metal` (all 8: matmulnbits, gqa, norm, softmax, rope, elementwise, data_movement,
  gather_block_quantized) + `cmake/metal_kernels.inc.in`.
- Hand-kernel machinery in `src/ep/ep.cc`: `AddKernel`/`CocoKernel`/`MarietteKernel`,
  `KernelBase`/`IOTensor`, the M-based prefill/decode hybrid routing, `NodePlan`, the whole
  `ComputeIO`/batched-dispatch path.
- Metal-shader compile/pipeline-registry/encode/batching in `src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}` (stripped
  to a minimal shared-MTLBuffer allocator).
- `src/ep/mlx_backend.cc` stub build + `Enabled()`/`Available()` + all `ORT_MPS_HAS_MLX`/
  `ORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX`/`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX` gating (MLX is always compiled, always on).
- `src/ops/` (obsolete hand-kernel `KernelBase` op-registry scaffold) and
  `src/dtype/dtype_traits.h` (hand-kernel MSL dtype-specialization scaffold).
- `tests/kernels/` (`kernel_test.cc`, `mariette_kernel_test.cc`, `ort_reference_test.py`) and the
  `mps_kernel_tests` / `mps_mariette_tests` ctest targets.

## What was KEPT
- `src/ep/mlx_backend.{h,cc}` — the ONNX→MLX translator (now the sole compute path, un-gated).
- `src/ep/ep.cc` — claim → convex-cluster → fuse → Compile(ONNX→MLX NodeDesc) → RunPlan.
- `src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}` — minimal MTLBuffer allocator for ORT device I/O + KV binding.
  **The MRR `[release]` leak fix in `Free`/dtor is PRESERVED** (the `=nil` MRR leak that crashed the
  machine is NOT reintroduced).
- Plugin-EP ABI (`CreateEpFactories`/`ReleaseEpFactory` in `entry.cc`), factory, allocator,
  data-transfer, KV-cache/IO-binding.

## New MLX-native architecture
`ONNX fused decoder subgraph → MLX graph → single mlx_eval at the subgraph boundary → ORT outputs`,
for BOTH prefill and decode. Claim set is now **⊆ MLX-translatable** (no fallback within the EP; an
unclaimed op runs on ORT's CPU EP; a claimed-but-untranslatable op is a hard `ORT_EP_FAIL`).

ONNX op → MLX translation:
| ONNX op (domain) | MLX target |
|---|---|
| MatMulNBits (com.microsoft, int4 blk32) | `mlx_quantized_matmul` (weights repacked+cached once) |
| GroupQueryAttention (com.microsoft, RoPE in-op) | `fast_scaled_dot_product_attention` + `fast_rope` |
| RMSNormalization (ai.onnx) | `fast_rms_norm` |
| SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization (com.microsoft) | skip-add + `fast_rms_norm` |
| GatherBlockQuantized (com.microsoft, **symmetric int4** only) | gather + int4 dequant |
| Softmax / Add / Mul / Sub / Sigmoid / Cast | matching MLX elementwise ops |

No-longer-claimed ops (were in the old hand claim set; now fall back to CPU): Div, SiLU, Swish, Gelu,
standalone RotaryEmbedding, Reshape, Transpose, Concat — the cpu-recipe decoder's claimed subgraph
does not need them (GetCapability still claims **390 / 393 nodes** as ONE fused subgraph).

## Verification (all green)
- `brew install mlx-c` → mlx 0.32.0, mlx-c 0.6.0 (`brew --prefix mlx-c` = `/opt/homebrew/opt/mlx-c`).
- **Build:** `cmake -S . -B build -G "Unix Makefiles" && cmake --build build -j8` → green, produces
  `build/libonnxruntime_mlx_ep.dylib`. CMake FAILS if mlx-c is absent (hard dep confirmed).
- **ctest 3/3 green:** `mlx_op_tests` (20 ONNX→MLX op-correctness checks vs ORT CPU, tolerance-gated),
  `mlx_e2e` (coherence), `mlx_leak_test`.
- **E2E coherence:** full-MLX prefill+decode emits `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13` =
  "**The capital of France is Paris.**"; matches ORT CPU exactly for the first 14 tokens, then the
  accepted fp32 decode drift. (ctest `mlx_e2e` uses num_new=8 to stay under drift for an exact gate.)
- **Perf (warm, ≥2 discard runs; M1 Max):**
  - Prefill/TTFT: 26-tok prompt **~15 ms** (MLX) vs ~33 ms (CPU) = **2.2×**; 512-tok prompt
    **~165 ms** vs ~575 ms (CPU) = **3.3–3.5×** (meets the 2.5–3× target; scales with prompt length).
  - Decode: **~122–148 tok/s** short ctx, ~56 tok/s at 512 ctx. Consistent with Phase-0's 1.02–1.09×
    vs the (now-deleted) hand path.
- **Memory:** leak test flat — growth from baseline **0.00 MiB** across all 7 bounded cycles (active
  1022.8 MiB → after-destroy 515.28 MiB every cycle). MRR safety preserved.

## Residual risk / follow-ups
- **No hand-kernel fallback.** A claimed op MLX can't translate is now a hard `ORT_EP_FAIL` (session
  fails) instead of silently degrading. Mitigation: claim set is pinned ⊆ MLX-translatable and
  `BuildPlan` re-checks `Supported()`. Any new op must be added to BOTH the claim logic and the
  translator together.
- **Decode is at parity, not a win**, on this 0.5B model (MLX decode ≈ CPU decode; MLX slower than
  CPU at long ctx). The pivot's justification is single-impl simplicity + prefill dominance +
  never-slower-than-hand decode, not a decode speedup. Larger models should widen the MLX decode edge.
- **GatherBlockQuantized asymmetric (`zero_points`, 4-input) is NOT translated** — MLX path assumes
  symmetric zp=8; the 4-input form is left unclaimed (CPU). Follow-up: implement MLX zero_points.
- **bf16 / fp16 activations:** current translated path is fp32-activation (matches cpu-recipe).
  Follow-up: bf16 end-to-end via MLX to cut the fp32 decode drift and memory.
- **`include/onnxruntime_mps/` dir + `MetalEp*` class names + `ORT_MPS_*` macros** retained (EP name
  must stay `MetalEP`; renaming is cosmetic churn with ABI risk). Optional future cleanup.

## Files changed (summary)
- Rewrote: `CMakeLists.txt`, `src/ep/metal_context.{h,mm}`, `src/ep/ep.{h,cc}`,
  `src/ep/mlx_backend.{h,cc}`, `include/onnxruntime_mps/version.h`, `README.md`, `docs/DESIGN.md`,
  `docs/OP_ARCHITECTURE.md`, `tests/README.md`, `tests/run_with_metal_validation.sh`.
- Added: `tests/ops/mlx_op_test.py`.
- Deleted: `src/kernels/`, `src/ops/`, `src/dtype/`, `tests/kernels/`, `cmake/metal_kernels.inc.in`.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-mlx-op-registry.md -->
# Decision: MLX EP modular opset-aware dtype-generic op-translation registry

**Author:** Nabil (EP architect)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mlx` (HEAD was 206f3fe; NOT committed — coordinator commits after validating green)
**Scope:** The FOUNDATION for full Mobius op coverage. This is the base other agents extend to cover every op.

---

## Summary

Replaced the monolithic `Translate()` if-chain + `Supported()` list in `mlx_backend.cc` with a
**single modular registry** keyed by `(domain, op_type, [min_opset, max_opset]) → handler`. Both the
claim-time membership check and the run-time translator consult the SAME table, so "claimed" can
never disagree with "translatable". Handlers are split into per-family modules under `src/ep/ops/`.
Threaded the ONNX opset (`since_version`) into `NodeDesc` and added a full ONNX→MLX dtype mapping so
the elementwise/activation/softmax/normalization/cast paths now run in **fp32, fp16 AND bf16** with a
single implementation. The 11 existing ops were migrated unchanged (pure refactor; e2e byte-identical).

---

## 1. Registry key design

```
struct OpRegistration { string domain; string op_type; int min_opset; int max_opset; OpHandler handler; };
using OpHandler = void (*)(TranslationContext& ctx, const NodeDesc& node);
```

- **domain**: `""` = `ai.onnx`, or `"com.microsoft"` for contrib ops.
- **op_type**: the ONNX op name.
- **opset range** `[min_opset, max_opset]`, inclusive. `kAnyOpset` (`-1`) = unbounded on that side.
  Version-insensitive / contrib ops register `{kAnyOpset, kAnyOpset}`. A version-split op registers
  two handlers with adjacent non-overlapping ranges (e.g. `[1,22]` and `[23,kAnyOpset]`).
- `OpRegistry::Find(domain, op, since_version)` returns the handler whose domain+op match and whose
  range contains `since_version`, else `nullptr`. `Supported()` = `Find() != nullptr`.

**Live example of the seam:** `RMSNormalization` is registered `[23, kAnyOpset]` (ONNX introduced it
at opset 23). Verified `GetSinceVersion()` returns 23 for an opset-24 model, so it matches. This is
the exact mechanism that will later route `Attention` opset-23 vs opset-24 and `TensorScatter`
(opset-24-only) to different handlers.

## 2. Opset threading

- `NodeDesc` gained `int since_version`.
- `ep.cc` node-build loop populates it from `Ort::ConstNode::GetSinceVersion()`.
- `ep.cc::NodeClaimable` is now AND-gated by `ort_mps_mlx::Supported(domain, op, since_version)` — a
  node must pass a family claim predicate AND have a registry handler. Shared table = no disagreement.
- Observed `since_version`: ai.onnx ops report real versions (Add=14, Cast=24, Softmax=13,
  RMSNormalization=23, Sigmoid=13); com.microsoft contrib ops report 1.

## 3. dtype mapping + bf16 status

`mlx_dtype MlxDtypeFromOnnx(ONNXTensorElementDataType)` in `src/ep/mlx_engine.h` / `mlx_backend.cc`:

| ONNX | MLX | ONNX | MLX |
|---|---|---|---|
| FLOAT | FLOAT32 | INT8/16/32/64 | INT8/16/32/64 |
| FLOAT16 | FLOAT16 | UINT8/16/32/64 | UINT8/16/32/64 |
| **BFLOAT16** | **BFLOAT16** | BOOL | BOOL |
| DOUBLE | FLOAT64 | (other) | FLOAT32 (fallback) |

Used in `Resolve`/`Bind`, constant materialization, the pre-eval boundary cast, and `CopyOut`
(memcpy cases added for bf16, bool, all int/uint widths, fp64). Because MLX carries the resolved
dtype through its ops, the **dtype-generic handlers need zero per-dtype branches**.

**Working dtypes today:**
- **fp32 / fp16 / bf16**: Add, Mul, Sub, Sigmoid, Softmax, Cast, RMSNormalization,
  SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization. (Cast: float↔float among the three + int64→int32.)
- **fp32 only** (unchanged): MatMulNBits, GroupQueryAttention, GatherBlockQuantized — quant repack /
  SDPA paths match the cpu-recipe graph. Widening these is follow-up.
