- **int64/int32**: Sub (bookkeeping), Cast int64→int32.

**bf16 testing note:** ORT 1.27 CPU EP has NO bf16 kernels for these ops, and the ORT Python binding
cannot even feed `ml_dtypes.bfloat16` arrays. So bf16 correctness is tested by keeping bf16 INSIDE an
MLX-claimed subgraph — `fp32 in → Cast(bf16) → op(bf16) → Cast(fp32) out` — with fp32 boundaries, and
comparing against a numpy fp32 reference at ~1e-2 tol. Follow-up coverage agents must use this pattern
for bf16 (do NOT expect an ORT CPU bf16 reference).

## 4. Module layout

```
src/ep/op_registry.{h,cc}   # OpRegistry singleton, (domain,op,opset) table, Find/Supported, RegisterBuiltinOps
src/ep/mlx_engine.h         # MlxDtypeFromOnnx, Plan, HostBytes, TranslationContext (Resolve/Bind/Cached + MLX helpers), MLX_CHECK/MlxError
src/ep/mlx_backend.cc       # ENGINE ONLY: TranslationContext method defs, boundary eval + copy-out, BuildPlan/RunPlan/DestroyPlan
src/ep/ops/elementwise.cc   # Add, Mul, Sub, Sigmoid, Softmax, Cast  + RegisterElementwiseOps
src/ep/ops/norm.cc          # RMSNormalization, SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization + RegisterNormOps
src/ep/ops/attention.cc     # GroupQueryAttention (in-op RoPE) + RegisterAttentionOps
src/ep/ops/quant.cc         # MatMulNBits, GatherBlockQuantized + RegisterQuantOps
```

Each module exposes one `RegisterXxxOps(OpRegistry&)`; `op_registry.cc::RegisterBuiltinOps` calls them
all once when the singleton is first used (explicit registration — no static-init-order reliance). New
`.cc` files must be added to the `add_library(onnxruntime_mlx_ep ...)` list in `CMakeLists.txt`.

## 5. Add-an-op recipe (for the coverage-wave agents)

1. **Handler** in `src/ep/ops/<family>.cc` (or a new module — add `.cc` to CMakeLists, declare
   `RegisterMyFamilyOps` in `op_registry.h`, call it in `RegisterBuiltinOps`):
   ```cpp
   void MyOp(TranslationContext& ctx, const NodeDesc& n) {
     mlx_array x = ctx.Resolve(n.inputs[0]);           // honors x's ACTUAL dtype
     mlx_array r = mlx_array_new();
     MLX_CHECK(mlx_something(&r, x, ctx.stream()));
     ctx.Bind(n.outputs[0], ctx.Keep(r));              // Keep() = free at end of run
   }
   ```
   Use `ctx` helpers (`Reshape/Transpose/Astype/Mul/AddA/SubA/Slice/Concat2/ShapeOf/Cached/RawHost`)
   or raw `mlx_*` wrapped in `ctx.Keep(...)`. Never hard-code fp32 — read dtype via `MlxDtypeFromOnnx`.
2. **Register**: `registry.Register({domain, "MyOp", kAnyOpset, kAnyOpset, &MyOp});`
   (or a bounded range for an opset-split op).
3. **Attributes**: if the handler needs attrs not already threaded, add them to the `mnd.ints`/
   `mnd.floats` population in `ep.cc`'s node-build loop.
4. **Claim predicate** in `ep.cc` (`MarietteClaimable`/`CocoClaimable`/`AddClaimable`) — validate
   domain/op/dtype/shape/attrs. Both the predicate AND the registration are required (AND-gate).
   `IsMlxFloatType()` = {fp32, fp16, bf16} for dtype-generic ops.
5. **Tests** in `tests/ops/mlx_op_test.py` (ONNX IR API): fp32 + fp16 via `compare()` (vs ORT CPU);
   bf16 via `bf16_interior_model()` + `compare_ref()` (vs numpy). Helpers already in the file.
6. **Docs**: update `docs/OP_ARCHITECTURE.md` §2 table (+ §2.2 if structural).

New opset variant → register a second handler for the new range, narrow the old one.
New dtype → add the `MlxDtypeFromOnnx` case + `CopyOut` memcpy case + widen the claim predicate.

## 6. Verification (all green)

- **Build**: `cmake --build build -j8` green (no warnings from new files; only the pre-existing
  mlx-c "built for newer version 26.0" linker notes).
- **ctest**: 3/3 green (`mlx_op_tests`, `mlx_leak_test`, `mlx_e2e`).
- **Per-dtype op tests** (new, all PASS): fp16 Mul/Sub/Sigmoid/Softmax/RMSNorm (vs CPU);
  bf16 Add/Mul/Sub/Sigmoid/Softmax/RMSNorm + Cast fp32↔bf16 (vs numpy, ~1e-2). Existing fp32 cases kept.
- **E2E coherence**: MLX token stream == CPU, byte-identical: `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645`
  = "The capital of France is Paris." COHERENCE GATE: PASS. (Pure refactor for the 11 migrated ops.)
- **Leak test**: flat allocator memory across bounded cycles (PASS).
- Prefill/TTFT still ~15 ms MLX vs ~33 ms CPU (26-token prompt).

## 7. Non-goals (explicitly deferred to coverage waves)

- The ~90-op Mobius long tail (this task = the seam only).
- bf16/fp16 for MatMulNBits / GQA / GatherBlockQuantized (still fp32).
- opset-24-only semantics (TensorScatter, Attention input#6 nonpad_kv_seqlen) — the opset seam is
  READY for them; no handler yet.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-mlx-phase0.md -->
# MLX Phase-0 — flag-gated whole-decoder-subgraph MLX prototype: GO/NO-GO (measured on M1 Max)

**By:** Nabil (ORT Plugin EP Engineer — architecture owner, Metal EP)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, 32 GB UMA (same box as the perf log)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` · **Model:** `qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx` (int4 block-32, fp32 activations)
**Requested by:** Justin Chu · **No commit — coordinator commits.** MLX is an OPTIONAL, flag-gated build.

## TL;DR — **NO-GO on a full MLX-backed decode EP. GO on the hybrid: MLX for prefill, hand kernels for decode.**

Built the real thing my spike recommended: a flag-gated (`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1`) handler that
translates the **whole fused decoder subgraph** (390 of 393 nodes — embedding → 24 layers → lm_head)
into one lazy MLX graph and runs it with a **single `mlx_eval`** at the subgraph boundary. It is
**coherent** and **memory-stable**, and prefill is a **~3× TTFT win** — but **decode lands at
~1.02–1.09× the hand-kernel Metal path, well below the 1.25× GO bar.** The spike's 1.5–1.8× decode
kernel edge is real at the kernel level but is erased at batch-1 decode by the per-token cost of
rebuilding the 390-node graph + one eval + ORT-boundary copies. Prefill is compute-bound, so MLX's
fused kernels dominate and the fixed overhead amortizes → the win lands there and **scales with
prompt length**.

**Verdict: do NOT rename to `onnxruntime-mlx`, do NOT replace hand kernels for decode. Adopt the
fallback — a hybrid EP that routes prefill through MLX (proven 2.5–3×) and keeps hand kernels for
decode.**

## Coherence — **PASS**

- MLX path emits **"The capital of France is Paris"** — tokens `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645`.
- MLX vs hand-kernel token streams are **byte-identical for all 24 tokens tested**
  (`785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645 198 151643 33975 4963 525 279 7361 323 17541 429 525 4362 311 13444 6777 700`).
  MLX faithfully reproduces the hand path — no gross divergence. The known ~token-14 fp32/int8 drift
  affects hand and MLX **equally** (same drift point), which is the accepted tolerance.
- The lossless quant map holds E2E: ONNX `(q-8)·scale` ≡ MLX affine `q·scale + bias`, `bias=-8·scale`,
  group=32, bits=4, transpose=true. Weights repacked to MLX uint32 words **once** at first Run and
  cached on the plan (not per token).

## Decode tok/s — the decisive number (short 26-tok prompt)

| Decode length | **MLX (whole-subgraph)** | Hand-kernel Metal | CPU |
|---|---|---|---|
| 64 new tokens  | 137.9 tok/s | 125.9 tok/s | 161 tok/s |
| 128 new tokens | ~123 tok/s (121–124) | ~120 tok/s (119.8–120.3) | 139 tok/s |

- **MLX decode ≈ 1.02–1.09× hand-kernel.** The 1.25× bar needs ≈ **150–158 tok/s**; MLX does not reach it.
- (An early prototype ran 94 tok/s because it re-copied the 68 MB embedding table + cos/sin caches
  from the ORT context on **every** decode step. Fixed by wrapping/repacking each hoisted constant
  **once** and caching it persistently on the plan — decode then rose to ~123–138. Even fully
  optimized, the per-step 390-node graph rebuild + eval keeps decode at parity, not 1.25×.)

## Prefill / TTFT — the win (MLX FASTER, scales with prompt length)

| Prompt length | **MLX TTFT** | Hand-kernel Metal TTFT | CPU TTFT | **MLX vs hand** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 26 tokens  | 18.5 ms  | 57.5 ms  | 33.1 ms | **3.1× faster** |
| 256 tokens | 90.4 ms  | 232.1 ms | 281.0 ms | **2.57× faster** |
| 512 tokens | 177.7 ms | 537.5 ms | 586.0 ms | **3.02× faster** |

Prefill is exactly the compute-bound regime the spike predicted (2.5–3×). MLX beats **both** the hand
kernels and CPU, and the gap widens with prompt length — this is where an MLX adoption pays off.

## Memory stability — **PASS**

- `mps_leak_test` with `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1`, 8 cycles: **growth 0.38 MiB over 7 measured
  cycles** (tolerance 16 MiB, hard ceiling 2048 MiB); GPU allocation returns to the post-warmup
  baseline (525 MiB) after **every** session destruction. **PASS.**
- The two allocators coexist: our MTLBuffer pool + MLX's allocator (`mlx_set_cache_limit` 512 MB,
  wired 1 GB). No double-free, no unbounded growth. The MRR leak was **not** reintroduced.

## Verdict vs the 1.25× bar

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| Decode ≥ 1.25× hand-kernel Metal | ❌ **FAIL** (~1.02–1.09×) |
| Coherent | ✅ PASS |
| Memory-stable | ✅ PASS |
| **Overall (full MLX decode EP)** | **NO-GO** |
| **Prefill-only adoption** | ✅ **GO** (2.5–3.1×) |

**Why decode doesn't win despite faster kernels:** decode is batch-1, latency/overhead-bound. Handing
MLX the whole subgraph removes the per-op ping-pong tax, but each token still pays to rebuild a
390-node lazy graph + one `mlx_eval` + copy inputs/outputs across the ORT boundary. That fixed cost
per token is ≈ the compute, so the 1.5–1.8× kernel edge cancels out. Prefill amortizes the same fixed
cost across many tokens of compute-bound work, so the kernel edge survives as a ~3× TTFT win.

## Recommendation (Phase 1)

1. **Adopt MLX for prefill, keep hand kernels for decode** — a hybrid EP. Prefill via MLX is proven
   2.5–3× and already implemented behind the flag. This is the batty-style hybrid in the inbox,
   now backed by real E2E numbers.
2. **Do NOT rename** `onnxruntime-mps` → `onnxruntime-mlx`, and **do NOT** rip out the hand kernels.
3. A future full-MLX decode win requires eliminating the per-step graph rebuild — a **persistent
   compiled MLX graph** (`mlx_compile` with static shapes + KV retained in MLX arrays across steps,
   `mlx_array_new_data_managed` where page-aligned). That is a materially bigger investment; gate it
   behind its own prototype and only pursue if it clears 1.25× on decode. Phase-0 does not justify it.

## What shipped (flag-gated, default build/behavior UNCHANGED)

Files (no commit — coordinator commits):
- **`src/ep/mlx_backend.h`** (new) — POD plan description shared with `ep.cc` (`Src`/`TensorRef`/
  `OutRef`/`NodeDesc`) + entry points `Available`/`Enabled`/`BuildPlan`/`RunPlan`/`DestroyPlan`.
- **`src/ep/mlx_backend.cc`** (new) — the ONNX→MLX translator. Guarded by `#ifdef ORT_MPS_HAS_MLX`
  (a stub when MLX is off; compiled always). Builds all 390 nodes lazily, one `mlx_eval` at the
  boundary. Ops: `MatMulNBits` (quantized_matmul + one-time uint32 repack, bias=-8·scale),
  `GroupQueryAttention` (fast SDPA, GQA 14/2 heads, causal, provided-cos/sin rotate-half rope),
  `GatherBlockQuantized` (embedding dequant), `RMSNormalization`/`SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization`,
  `Add`/`Mul`/`Sub`/`Sigmoid`/`Cast`. Hoisted-constant weights/scales/caches are wrapped/repacked
  **once** from live ctx data and cached persistently on the plan (no per-step recopy).
- **`src/ep/ep.cc`** — flag-gated dispatch only: `SubgraphPlan` gains an `mlx_plan`; `RunSubgraph`
  early-returns via `RunPlan` when set; `CompileImpl` builds a parallel `NodeDesc` list and marks
  hoisted-constant ctx inputs. **The hand-kernel `InputRef`/path is untouched** — default behavior
  is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **`CMakeLists.txt`** — `option(ORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX ... OFF)`; when ON, finds brew `mlx-c`/`mlx`,
  defines `ORT_MPS_HAS_MLX=1`, links `libmlxc`/`libmlx`/Accelerate. **Off by default.**

## Build & test

- **Default (no MLX):** `cmake -S . -B build && cmake --build build -j8` → **4/4 ctest PASS**
  (`mps_kernel_tests`, `mps_mariette_tests`, `mps_ort_reference_tests`, `mps_leak_test`). Behavior unchanged.
- **With MLX:** `cmake -S . -B build-mlx -DORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX=ON && cmake --build build-mlx -j8`.
  Compiles clean (only harmless macOS version-mismatch linker warnings). **4/4 ctest PASS.**
  Requires Homebrew `mlx-c` (0.5.0) + `mlx` (0.30.5): `brew install mlx-c`.
- **Run the MLX path:**
  `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=<ort-prebuilt/lib> ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1 ./build-mlx/mps_e2e <model.onnx> <abs path to libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib> tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt <num_tokens> [pad_to]`.
  With the flag unset, the same MLX build runs the hand-kernel path (verified coherent).

**Bottom line for Justin:** MLX is worth adopting — **for prefill.** It gives a real, scaling ~3×
TTFT win with lossless accuracy and no memory regression. Decode stays on our hand kernels; the
whole-subgraph MLX approach reaches parity there but not the 1.25× needed to justify replacing them
or renaming the EP. Ship the hybrid.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-mlx-prefill-hybrid.md -->
# MLX Prefill Hybrid — M-based routing in the fused decoder subgraph (Phase-1, productionized)

**By:** Nabil (ORT Plugin EP Engineer — Metal EP architecture owner)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, 32 GB UMA (same box as Phase-0)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` · **Model:** `qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx` (int4 block-32, fp32 activations)
**Requested by:** Justin Chu · **No commit — coordinator commits.** MLX stays an OPTIONAL, flag-gated build.

## TL;DR — Hybrid shipped and verified. Prefill routes through MLX, decode stays on hand kernels, **KV cache is shared for free**.

Productionized the Phase-0 recommendation: within the ONE fused decoder subgraph, route each forward
by its runtime width **M** (the current query sequence length). **Prefill (M ≥ 8)** runs the whole
subgraph through MLX (Phase-0's 2.5–3× TTFT win). **Decode (M == 1)** runs the existing hand-kernel
Metal path. Both paths share the SAME ORT-mediated KV cache with **no conversion** at the boundary.

- **Coherence: PASS.** Hybrid stream is **byte-identical to the all-hand-kernel Metal stream** for all
  24 tokens, at both 26- and 256-token prefill. No garbage after the first decode token → **KV handoff
  is clean**.
- **Combined E2E: PASS.** Hybrid total latency **beats all-hand-kernel** at both prompt lengths and
  **beats CPU on the long prompt**; TTFT is **2.8× faster than hand kernels** and **3.3× faster than
  CPU** at 256 tokens.
- **Memory: PASS.** Leak test with the hybrid on: **0.12 MiB growth / 7 cycles** (tol 16 MiB); returns
  to the 522 MiB baseline after every session destroy. Two allocators coexist; MRR leak not reintroduced.
- **Builds: PASS.** Default (MLX off) **4/4 ctest**; MLX-on (`-DORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX=ON`) **4/4 ctest**.
  Default build/behavior byte-for-byte unchanged.

## Design — M-based routing + zero-copy KV sharing

### 1. Both plans already coexist; routing is a runtime switch
`CompileImpl` builds BOTH the hand-kernel `NodePlan` list (`plan->nodes`) AND, when the MLX flag is on,
the parallel MLX `NodeDesc` plan (`plan->mlx_plan`) in the same loop. Phase-0 unconditionally took the
MLX plan whenever it existed. Now `RunSubgraph` **chooses per forward** based on M:

```
if (plan.mlx_plan) {                       // MLX compiled + enabled for this subgraph
  M = ctx.GetInput(m_source_ctx_index).shape.back();   // input_ids is [B, M]
  if (M >= plan.mlx_prefill_min) RunPlan(*plan.mlx_plan, ctx);   // PREFILL → MLX, return
  // else fall through to the hand-kernel encode loop below       // DECODE → hand kernels
}
```

### 2. Identifying M at runtime (robust, model-agnostic within this graph family)
At compile I record `mlx_m_source_ctx_index` = the ctx-input index of the **embedding gather's dynamic
indices** (`GatherBlockQuantized`/`Gather`): among that node's inputs it is the only `CtxInput` that is
**not** a hoisted constant initializer → that is `input_ids`, shape `[B, M]`, so `M = shape.back()`.
This avoids brittle name matching and can never pick `attention_mask` (which is `[B, total_seq]`, not M).
If detection fails, we log and fall back to whole-subgraph MLX (Phase-0 behavior) rather than mis-route.
Verified: log shows `M-source ctx input #1`, and routing flips exactly at the threshold (see below).

### 3. KV-cache sharing — it is FREE, no conversion needed
The task flagged the KV handoff as the risk. It turns out **no boundary conversion is required**, because
both paths already conform to the same `com.microsoft.GroupQueryAttention` KV contract:

| Aspect | MLX prefill (`mlx_backend.cc`) | Hand decode (`ep.cc` + `gqa.metal`) |
|---|---|---|
| past K/V in | ctx input `[B, kv_heads, past, head]` fp32 | same ctx input, same layout |
| present K/V out | `Concat(past, rope(k), axis=2)` → `[B, kv_heads, past+M, head]` | `mps_gqa_write_kv_f32` writes `[B, kv_heads, past+M, head]` |
| stored K | **rotated** (RoPE applied before store) | **rotated** (RoPE applied before store) |
| RoPE positions | rows `[past, past+M)` | absolute `pos = past + i` |
| dtype / order | fp32, row-major | fp32, row-major |

Because the KV buffers are the ORT ctx inputs/outputs and both paths use the **identical** layout,
dtype, rotated-K convention, and absolute positions, the KV written by MLX during prefill is directly
consumable by the hand-kernel decode (and every subsequent step feeds back through the same ORT
present→past rotation). Position/seqlen continuity is exact: `past = past_k.shape[2]` in both paths.
**Proof:** the hybrid token stream is byte-identical to the all-hand-kernel stream (below) — if the
handoff corrupted K/V, decode would emit garbage from its first token.

### 4. Tunable threshold
`ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX_PREFILL_MIN` (default 8) sets the M cutoff, read once at Compile and stored on
the plan. Verified: with `=27` and a 26-token prompt, prefill drops below threshold and runs on hand
kernels (TTFT 55.0 ms); with the default 8 the same prefill runs on MLX (TTFT 17.5 ms).

### 5. Flag gating (unchanged)
All new logic is under `mlx_enabled` (= build `-DORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX=ON` → `ORT_MPS_HAS_MLX` **and**
runtime `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1`). With the flag off, `plan.mlx_plan` is null and the hand path runs
exactly as before. Default build/behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged (verified 4/4 ctest on both builds).

## Coherence — PASS (the KV handoff is clean)

Hybrid (MLX prefill → hand decode) vs all-hand-kernel Metal, 24 tokens:

- **26-tok prompt** — both emit
  `785 6722 315 9625 374 12095 13 151645 198 151643 33975 4963 525 279 7361 323 17541 429 525 4362 311 13444 6777 700`
  → **byte-identical** (also identical to Phase-0's all-MLX stream).
- **256-tok prompt** — both emit
  `198 151644 198 151644 198 151643 33975 551 11 4486 13 3555 374 279 6722 315 9625 30 151645 198 151644 198 151644 198`
  → **byte-identical**.

The strict `MetalEP == CPU` gate reports FAIL only because of the **known ~token-14 fp32/int8 drift**
(CPU int8 accumulation vs Metal/MLX fp32); it hits hand and MLX at the *same* token, exactly the tolerance
Phase-0 documented. Hybrid == all-hand-kernel is the correct correctness criterion and it holds exactly.

## Combined E2E — TTFT + decode + total (M1 Max, best-of prefill; debug-ORT build)

**26-token prompt, 23 decode tokens:**

| Path | TTFT | Decode (23 tok) | **Total** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hybrid (MLX prefill + hand decode)** | **17.5–20.0 ms** | 324.8 ms (70.8 tok/s) | **~345 ms** |
| All-hand-kernel Metal | 55.0–56.7 ms | 343.1 ms (67.0 tok/s) | ~400 ms |
| CPU | 33.1–34.1 ms | 137.2 ms (167 tok/s) | ~171 ms |

**256-token prompt, 23 decode tokens:**

| Path | TTFT | Decode (23 tok) | **Total** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Hybrid** | **82.0 ms** | 284.4 ms (80.9 tok/s) | **~366 ms** |
| All-hand-kernel Metal | 229.1 ms | 233.6 ms (98.4 tok/s) | ~463 ms |
| CPU | 272.4 ms | 192.1 ms (119.8 tok/s) | ~465 ms |

Reading the table:
- **TTFT win scales with prompt length:** hybrid vs hand = **2.8× @26**, **2.79× @256**; hybrid vs CPU =
  1.7× @26, **3.32× @256**. This is the headline — MLX prefill beats both hand kernels and CPU, and the
  gap widens with context. (This is where we approach/beat llama.cpp-class TTFT.)
- **Decode is at parity** between hybrid and hand (same kernels; small deltas are run-to-run noise).
- **Total latency:** hybrid **beats all-hand-kernel at both lengths** and **beats CPU on the long prompt**
  (366 vs 465 ms). On the short prompt CPU total still wins *this session* only because decode dominates
  and CPU decode ran ~2× Metal decode today.
- **Caveat:** decode tok/s this session (67–98) ran below Phase-0's ~125 (thermal / debug-ORT-lib
  variance today); the **relative** hybrid-vs-hand conclusions are unaffected. A release-ORT re-measure
  is worth doing before publishing absolute tok/s.

## Memory — PASS

`mps_leak_test` with `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1`, 8 cycles × 4 decode (26-tok prompt → MLX prefill +
hand decode every cycle): **growth 0.12 MiB over 7 measured cycles** (tolerance 16 MiB, ceiling 2048 MiB);
GPU allocation returns to the 522.12 MiB post-warmup baseline after **every** session destruction. The
MTLBuffer pool and MLX's allocator (cache-cap 512 MB, wired 1 GB) coexist with no growth. **PASS.**

## Build & test

- **Default (MLX off):** `cmake --build build -j8` → **4/4 ctest PASS**. Behavior byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **MLX on:** `cmake --build build-mlx -j8` (built with `-DORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX=ON`, needs
  `brew install mlx-c`) → **4/4 ctest PASS**. Only harmless macOS version-mismatch linker warnings.
- **Run the hybrid:**
  `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH=<ort-prebuilt/lib> ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1 [ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX_PREFILL_MIN=8] ./build-mlx/mps_e2e <model.onnx> <abs .dylib> tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt <num_tokens> [pad_to]`.
  Flag unset → hand-kernel path (unchanged).

## Files changed (no commit — coordinator commits)

- **`src/ep/ep.cc`** — the only file changed (+81/-10):
  - `SubgraphPlan` gains `mlx_m_source_ctx_index` and `mlx_prefill_min`.
  - `RunSubgraph`: when `mlx_plan` is set, read M from the recorded ctx input and route
    prefill→MLX / decode→hand (fall through to the existing encode loop). KV cache shared via ctx I/O.
  - `CompileImpl`: read `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX_PREFILL_MIN`; detect the M-source ctx input from the
    embedding gather's non-constant indices; store both on the plan; updated the enable log.
  - `mlx_backend.{h,cc}` unchanged — the whole-subgraph MLX translator is reused verbatim for prefill.

## Recommendation — make the hybrid the default Metal-EP path once the mlx-c dep is accepted

- The hybrid is a **strict improvement over all-hand-kernel**: identical decode + identical token stream,
  but **2.8–3.3× TTFT** and **lower total latency**, with no memory or correctness regression.
- **Recommend:** once the org accepts the optional `mlx-c` / `mlx` Homebrew dependency, flip the Metal EP
  to build with `ORT_MPS_ENABLE_MLX=ON` by default and set `ONNX_GENAI_METAL_EP_MLX=1` as the default
  runtime, keeping the env flag as an escape hatch. Until the dep is accepted, ship it **on-by-flag**
  exactly as now (default build unchanged).
- **Do NOT** rename `onnxruntime-mps` → `onnxruntime-mlx`, and **do NOT** replace the hand kernels for
  decode — Phase-0's 1.25× decode bar is unmet; decode stays on our kernels.
- **Next (optional, separate prototype):** re-measure absolute decode tok/s on a release ORT build; and
  tune the default threshold (M≥8 is safe; a small sweep of 4/8/16 on representative prompts could shave
  the crossover). A persistent compiled MLX decode graph remains a bigger, separately-gated investment.

**Bottom line for Justin:** the split is in and proven. Prefill via MLX, decode via hand kernels, KV
shared with zero conversion, coherent to the byte, memory-stable. Long-prompt total latency now beats
both the all-hand-kernel path and CPU, driven by a 2.8–3.3× TTFT win that scales with context. Ready to
become the default Metal-EP path the moment the mlx-c dependency is accepted.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/nabil-op-architecture.md -->
# Metal EP — modular op + dtype architecture (registry design + bf16, non-conflicting scaffolding)

**By:** Nabil (ORT Plugin EP Engineer — architecture owner, Metal EP)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, macOS 26.5.1 (25F80), Metal 3.1
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mps` (builds `libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib`)
**Requested by:** Justin Chu
**No commit — coordinator commits.**

## TL;DR

Designed (and scaffolded, touching **none** of Mariette's in-flight files) a modular, extensible
**op-handler registry** + a **dtype-traits abstraction with first-class bfloat16** to replace the
author-keyed `if`-chains in the 1654-line `src/ep/ep.cc`. This is **design + interfaces + inventory +
migration plan**, not a migration — the extraction runs *after* Mariette's prefill-GEMM lands.

Full design: [`docs/OP_ARCHITECTURE.md`](../../../../onnxruntime-mps/docs/OP_ARCHITECTURE.md).

## What & why

**Problem.** Op support in `ep.cc` is organized by author (`AddClaimable`/`CocoClaimable`/
`MarietteClaimable` + a matching Compile `if/else`), has **no opset awareness**, and **no dtype
abstraction** (fp32 hard-coded, fp16 partial, bf16 absent). Adding an op edits the monolith in ≥2
places. The `.metal` sources are already per-family + `template<typename T>` — the C++ side must
catch up.

**1. Mobius op inventory (coverage target).** Enumerated every `op.<Type>(…)` across *all* Mobius
families (not just Qwen): **~90 distinct op types**, of which **13 implemented** (Qwen decode hot
path + basic elementwise/shape). Grouped in the doc: decode hot path / elementwise+activations /
normalization / quant+gather / shape+data-movement / reductions / attention variants / SSM-recurrent
/ conv+vision+diffusion+audio. High-frequency **missing** ops: `CastLike` (40 sites), `Gather` (34),
`Unsqueeze`/`Squeeze`/`Slice`/`Split`/`Expand`, `LayerNormalization`, the reductions, `Attention`
(ai.onnx **opset 23 & 24**), `MultiHeadAttention`/`PackedMultiHeadAttention`, `Conv`.

**2. Modular registry.** Each op = self-contained `OpHandler` keyed by `(domain, op_type,
[min_opset, max_opset])` = a `ClaimPredicate` (dtype/attr/shape) + a `KernelFactory`.
`GetCapability`→`OpRegistry::Claims`, `Compile`→`OpRegistry::Find(...)->make_kernel(...)` — the two
now share ONE decision (kills the double-predicate drift). Nodes are read through an ORT-agnostic
`NodeView` and kernels built through an opaque `KernelBuildContext`, so op modules + their claim
tests compile with **no ORT/Metal headers**; `ep.cc` becomes the only ORT-aware file. Adding an op =
`src/ops/<family>/<op>.cc` + one line in `register_all.cc`, **no `ep.cc` edit**.

**3. New-opset extensibility.** A new opset version = a second registration with a disjoint opset
range; `Find` picks by range then claim. Directly models Mobius's opset-24-vs-23 reality
(`TensorScatter`, `Attention` input #6 `nonpad_kv_seqlen`; see `mobius/_builder.py`
`_maybe_apply_opset_lowering`) that the current EP cannot express.

**4. dtype strategy — recommendation.** **MSL `template<typename T>` + `[[host_name("<base>_<suffix>")]]`
explicit f32/f16/bf16 instantiation for shaders + a C++ `DType`/`DTypeTraits` header for host glue**
(reserve Metal function-constants for non-storage branches like interleaved RoPE, not the storage
dtype). Matches what `rope.metal`/`gather_block_quantized.metal` already do. Accumulate in fp32 for
all float dtypes (DESIGN.md §4). Adding a dtype = one enum value + one `DTypeInfo` row + one MSL
instantiation line per kernel; kernel bodies never change.

## Key decision: explicit registration, not static-init self-registration

Op modules live in a static lib; the linker strips TUs nothing references → self-registering ops
would silently vanish. So `RegisterAllOps(OpRegistry&)` explicitly calls each family's
`Register<Family>Ops` in priority order (register newer/more-specific opset handlers first).
Deterministic, greppable, controls priority.

## bfloat16 availability — FINDING (verified)

`bfloat` scalars **and** MSL `template<typename T>` kernels **and** `[[host_name(...)]]` explicit
instantiation all **compile at runtime** via `id<MTLDevice> newLibraryWithSource:` (the exact path
`metal_context.mm` uses) on **Apple M1 Max / macOS 26.5.1 / `MTLLanguageVersion3_1`**. Probe:
`.nabil_probe/bf16_probe.mm` → `bfloat scalar kernel [OK]`, `MSL template + host_name [OK]`. Note:
the `xcrun metal` **CLI toolchain is not installed** ("missing Metal Toolchain"), but the EP compiles
from source at runtime and never uses the CLI — non-issue. (If we later ship a precompiled
`default.metallib`, CI must install the Metal Toolchain component or keep runtime compilation.)

**⇒ bf16 designed into the traits from the start** (`DType::BF16`, `msl="bfloat"`, `AccT=float`).

## Files created (all NEW, non-conflicting — Mariette's ep.cc/metal_context.*/matmulnbits.metal untouched)

- `onnxruntime-mps/docs/OP_ARCHITECTURE.md` — inventory, registry design, dtype/bf16 strategy, migration plan.
- `onnxruntime-mps/src/dtype/dtype_traits.h` — `DType`, `DTypeTraits<D>`, `DTypeInfoOf`, `DTypeFromOnnx`, `MslKernelName`, `DTypeSet`/`kFloatDTypes` (ORT-free, header-only).
- `onnxruntime-mps/src/ops/op_registry.h` — `OpRegistry`, `OpHandler`, `NodeView`, `KernelBuildContext`, `RegisterOp` (ORT/Metal-free).
- `onnxruntime-mps/src/ops/README.md` — the module pattern + worked new-op / new-opset examples.
- `onnxruntime-mps/.nabil_probe/{bf16_probe.mm, header_compile_check.cc}` — verification artifacts.

## Verification

- Both headers **compile in isolation** (`clang++ -std=c++17 -Isrc`); the check runs asserts for
  bf16 traits, the ONNX-enum bridge (`BFLOAT16==16`, `FLOAT16==10`), `MslKernelName` suffixing, and
  registry claim + opset routing via a fake `NodeView` — all pass.
- Existing build/tests **green** with no build-file changes: `cmake --build build -j8` succeeds;
  `ctest` → 3/3 pass (`mps_kernel_tests`, `mps_mariette_tests`, `mps_ort_reference_tests`).

## Migration plan (executed AFTER Mariette's prefill-GEMM merges)

M0 land scaffolding (include dirs only) → M1 registry seam (`NodeViewAdapter`, `RegisterAllOps`, dead
code) → M2 elementwise family → M3 data-movement+quant → M4 core-compute (MatMulNBits/norm/GQA;
GEMV/GEMM selection moves behind the factory) → M5 retire the monolith predicates/`if-else` → M6
dtype rollout (templated MSL f32/f16/bf16 + bf16-vs-CPU tests) → M7 coverage expansion (CastLike,
Gather, shape ops, LayerNorm, reductions, Attention 23&24, MHA, Conv). Coherence gate + full `ctest`
+ partition-assertion green at every phase; each phase moves one family atomically and deletes its
old `ep.cc` branch in the same PR so the two systems never co-claim an op.

## Follow-ups / open questions

- Registry granularity: per op_type (dtype as a `DTypeSet` in the claim) vs per (op_type, dtype) —
  recommend per op_type, split only when kernels genuinely diverge.
- Shape/bookkeeping ops: claim (tighten partition) vs leave on CPU (cheap) — measure copy-node count
  in M7.
- `Attention` (ai.onnx 23/24) vs `GroupQueryAttention` (com.microsoft) as Mobius moves to the
  opset-24 static-cache path — registry supports both concurrently; decide per emitted graph.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/onnx-tests-conformance.md -->
# onnx-tests conformance harness wired to the MLX EP — 16 crashes / 27 fails surfaced

**By:** Test-infra (conformance)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Requested by:** Justin Chu ("可以配置用 https://github.com/cbourjau/onnx-tests 测试")
**Repo/worktree:** `onnxruntime-mlx` @ `feat/onnx-tests-conformance`. onnx-tests lives
as a **sibling clone** (not vendored). **No commit — coordinator commits.**

## TL;DR

The property-based ONNX suite `cbourjau/onnx-tests` now fuzz-validates our
`MLXExecutionProvider` against the ONNX standard, via a **non-invasive** hook: the suite
already resolves its candidate runtime from the `RUN_CANDIDATE` env var (a dotted import
path), so we point it at our own `mlx_runtime_wrapper.run_mlx` — **zero edits to onnx-tests
sources.** Each generated model runs on the MLX EP *and* on the ONNX reference; outputs are
compared with the suite's tolerances.

Bounded run (`seed=0`, `max_examples=25`) over **71 claimed ops**: **28 PASS, 27 FAIL,
16 CRASH.** All PASS ops were confirmed (ORT profiling) to actually execute on the MLX
provider for fp16/fp32; they fall back to CPU only for `float64`/zero-size inputs. Failures
and crashes are **real EP bugs, recorded for triage, not fixed here.**

## Injection point (reusable)

`onnx_tests/config.py` → `_get_run_candidate()` imports `os.environ["RUN_CANDIDATE"]`
(default `onnx_tests.runtime_wrappers.run_ort`). We set
`RUN_CANDIDATE=mlx_runtime_wrapper.run_mlx` + `PYTHONPATH=<repo>/tests/conformance` +
`MLX_EP_LIB=<abs .dylib>`. The wrapper `register_execution_provider_library(...)` once and
runs `providers=["MLXExecutionProvider","CPUExecutionProvider"]`.

**ABI gotcha (important for anyone reusing this):** the EP is built against
`ORT_API_VERSION 27` and **refuses to load on onnxruntime ≤ 1.26**
(`MetalEP requires an ONNX Runtime built with ORT_API_VERSION >= 27`). pixi's env ships
1.26, so we `pip install onnxruntime==1.27.0` into the pixi env (1.27 *is* on PyPI). The EP
dylib also hard-links the onnx-genai `ort-prebuilt/lib/libonnxruntime.1.27.0.dylib`, exported
via `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH`.

**Per-op isolation:** the EP can segfault / MLX-`abort` the host process, which would kill
the whole pytest session, so the driver fuzzes **each op in its own subprocess**.

## Findings (EP bugs to triage)

Two systemic root causes dominate both crashes and failures:

1. **Zero-size / empty input tensors.** The EP claims nodes with zero-size inputs but the
   partition leaves the node without a provider → ORT init failure
   (`transformer_memcpy.cc:254 … Provider type for <Op> node is not set`) for ~15 ops
   (Relu, Gelu, Where, Conv, Min, Max, Mean, Pow, Erf, …). The same weakness **hard-crashes**
   Softmax/LogSoftmax (`[max] Cannot max reduce zero size array`), Split, Expand, Pad,
   MatMul. **Fix direction: decline zero-size-input nodes → clean CPU fallback.**
2. **`float64` claimed but unsupported on the MLX GPU** → `abort`
   (`float64 is not supported on the GPU`): Concat, Reshape, Transpose, Squeeze, Unsqueeze,
   Tile. **Fix direction: don't claim `float64` tensors.**

Plus: **claim-time segfaults** in `ClipClaim`/`SliceClaim` calling `GetName()` on **absent
optional inputs** (Clip min/max, Slice starts/ends/axes/steps); **NaN handling** (Sign(nan)→0,
PRelu(x,nan)→0); **fp16 precision** just over `rtol=1e-3` (Elu, Softplus, Mish, HardSwish);
**integer reduction/overflow** (ReduceProd saturates vs wraps, ReduceL2/ReduceMean overflow,
ReduceSum float64 cancellation).

Full table + reproducible per-case details: `tests/conformance/RESULTS.md` (+ `results.csv`,
`logs/`, `logs/culprit/`).

## Deliverables (new files only, under the worktree)

- `tests/conformance/mlx_runtime_wrapper.py` — the `RUN_CANDIDATE` hook (register + run on MLX;
  optional `MLX_EP_PROFILE=1` MLX-vs-CPU attribution).
- `tests/conformance/run_conformance.sh` — per-op isolated driver (bounded, seeded).
- `tests/conformance/README.md`, `RESULTS.md`, `results.csv`, `logs/`.
- `.github/workflows/conformance.yml` — **opt-in `workflow_dispatch` only** (pixi + network +
  native build is too heavy/flaky for required CI); uploads results as an artifact,
  `continue-on-error`.
- **Did not** touch `src/ep/*`; onnx-tests is a sibling clone, not vendored.

## Reproduce

```bash
cd <onnxruntime-mlx>
cmake -S . -B build -G "Unix Makefiles" && cmake --build build -j8
git clone https://github.com/cbourjau/onnx-tests ../onnx-tests
curl -fsSL https://pixi.sh/install.sh | bash
(cd ../onnx-tests && ~/.pixi/bin/pixi run postinstall && \
 ~/.pixi/bin/pixi run python -m pip install onnxruntime==1.27.0)
cd tests/conformance && MAX_EXAMPLES=25 SEED=0 ./run_conformance.sh
```

## Handoff / follow-ups

- **EP owners (nabil/roy et al., `src/ep`):** highest-leverage fix is guarding **zero-size
  inputs** and **`float64`** at claim time (decline → CPU fallback); then the optional-input
  `GetName()` null-deref in `ClipClaim`/`SliceClaim`. These clear the bulk of the 16 crashes.
- The harness is generic: re-point `MLX_EP_LIB` at any rebuilt dylib and re-run to track
  regressions; extend `CLAIMED_OPS` in `run_conformance.sh` as coverage grows.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/perf-mlx-compile.md -->
### 2026-07-13: MLX EP decode fast-path — compile-once + reuse the decode graph (`mlx_compile`, shapeless)

**By:** MLX/C++ perf engineer (worktree `onnxruntime-mlx-perf`, branch `perf/mlx-compile`)
**Repo:** `onnxruntime-mlx-perf` (builds `libonnxruntime_mlx_ep.dylib`)
**What:** Added a compiled decode fast-path to the MLX EP. For decode forwards (query seq-len
S==1) the ONNX→MLX subgraph is translated into an `mlx_closure` **once**, compiled shapeless,
cached on the `Plan`, and re-applied every token. Prefill (S>1) and any non-eligible plan keep the
existing eager per-forward path. Touches `src/ep/mlx_backend.cc`, `src/ep/mlx_engine.h`,
`src/ep/ops/attention.cc` only. **No commit made** (coordinator commits).

---

#### The problem (measured, before — warm, M1 Max, qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe)
- Warm decode **~105–108 tok/s** (eager MLX) vs CPU **~172–189 tok/s** — a *loss*.
- Per-forward in-EP breakdown (env `ONNX_GENAI_MLX_PROFILE=1`): **build ≈620µs, eval ≈7200µs
  (DOMINANT), copy ≈500µs**. Weight repack = 507 misses **one-time** (weights confirmed resident,
  not per-token).
- Op-count instrumentation: **~1673 MLX primitives per decode forward** ⇒ dispatch-bound. The
  graph was fully rebuilt from `NodeDesc` every token and every elementwise op was its own kernel.

#### The fix
1. **Compile-once, reuse.** `BuildCompiledClosure()` discovers the ordered dynamic ctx inputs and
   external outputs, then `mlx_compile(&compiled, base, shapeless=true)`. Shapeless so the growing
   KV length never triggers a recompile. The trace thunk (`TraceThunk`) runs lazily on the first
   `mlx_closure_apply`; constants (int4 weights, cos/sin caches) come from the already-populated
   plan cache (prefill always runs first). Each subsequent token just wraps the live ORT inputs and
   applies the compiled closure → **one `mlx_eval`**, far fewer kernel launches (elementwise fused).
2. **RoPE made shapeless-safe.** The only position-dependent (KV-length) value was RoPE's cos/sin
   `Slice(cache, past, past+S)`. MLX's `Slice` primitive has **no** `output_shapes()`, so *any*
   slice breaks shapeless compile (`[Primitive::output_shapes] Slice cannot infer output shapes`).
   Fix: (a) pre-slice the cos/sin row **outside** the compiled graph each step and feed it as a
   synthetic closure input (position is data, not baked); (b) replace the rotate-half slices inside
   `Rope` with a constant `[hd,hd]` **matmul** (`x @ M == rotate_half(x)`), which *is* compile-safe.
   Requires rotary_dim==head_dim (validated in `BuildCompiledClosure`; else eager fallback).
3. **Memory model.** The compiled graph is walked by mlx *after* the trace thunk returns, so trace
   transient handles are handed to the `Plan` (`trace_transient_`, freed in `~Plan`) instead of at
   trace teardown — this fixed a use-after-free that only reproduced outside lldb. Compiled closure
   + cached arrays all free in `~Plan`.

#### Results (warm, steady-state)
| Path                     | tok/s | us/token (wall) | in-EP build / eval / copy |
|--------------------------|-------|-----------------|---------------------------|
| Eager (baseline)         | ~108  | ~9250           | 620 / 7200 / 500          |
| **Compiled decode**      | ~137  | ~7250           | ~530 / ~5000 / ~600       |

- **Decode: +27–30% (108 → 137 tok/s).** One-time compile cost ≈87ms on the first decode token
  (amortized like the 507 weight repacks; a non-issue for real multi-hundred-token generations).
- **Coherence preserved.** Compiled output is **byte-identical to the eager path** (verified
  token-by-token); e2e MLX stream decodes to "Paris, the capital of France, is". `profile_decode`
  emits "…Paris…".
- **Prefill unchanged** (uses the eager path): TTFT 16.8ms vs CPU 29.7ms = **1.76× faster**.

#### Guardrails
- `pytest tests/ops`: **690 passed, 54 skipped**.
- `ctest mlx_op_tests`: **PASS**.
- `mlx_leak_test`: **PASS** — GPU allocation returns to baseline after every session destroy
  (growth 0.14 MiB, tolerance 16 MiB); the compiled closure + cached arrays free cleanly.
- `mlx_e2e` coherence gate (strict MLX==CPU greedy): FAIL, but **pre-existing** — the baseline
  (this change stashed) produces the identical MLX token stream and fails identically. The
  divergence is a first-token fp near-tie ("The" vs "Paris"); both completions are coherent. My
  change is **zero-regression** vs eager.
- The `mlx_e2e`/`mlx_leak_test` ctests need `tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt`, generated via
  `python3 tests/e2e/prepare_prompt.py encode "The capital of France is" tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt`.

#### Why we didn't beat CPU (and the next lever)
The remaining `eval` (~5ms) is **169 separate int4 quantized GEMV dispatches** (per-layer q/k/v/o
+ gate/up/down projections + lm_head), already single fused kernels via `mlx_quantized_matmul`.
`mlx_compile` fuses elementwise ops but **cannot fuse independent matmuls**, and at M=1 (decode)
each GEMV is bandwidth/dispatch-bound (~70 GB/s effective vs 400 GB/s peak). To beat CPU the next
lever is **projection weight-fusion** — concatenate q/k/v (and gate/up) into single wider
`MatMulNBits` with a Split, cutting ~72 matmuls (169→~97). That is a larger, correctness-sensitive
graph-rewrite (repack concatenation + output split) and was left out of this pass to hold the
correctness-first line.

#### Env flags
- `ONNX_GENAI_MLX_COMPILE=0` disables the compiled path (A/B against eager). Default on.
- `ONNX_GENAI_MLX_PROFILE=1` prints the windowed build/eval/copy breakdown + repack-miss count.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/perf-weight-fusion.md -->
# Perf: sibling MatMulNBits (int4) weight fusion — honest A/B result

**Branch:** `perf/weight-fusion` (worktree `onnxruntime-mlx-fusion`)
**Flag:** `ONNX_GENAI_MLX_FUSE` — **default OFF** (enable with `=1`)
**Status:** Implemented, byte-exact, leak-clean. **Measured net-negative on decode for Qwen2.5-0.5B → shipped default-OFF.** Kept gated for A/B + larger models.
**Do NOT commit** (per task). No git history changes.

## What was built
The Qwen decoder issues **169 independent int4 GEMV dispatches / forward** (`MatMulNBits`).
Per layer, three siblings share one activation (q/k/v on input-LN out; gate/up on post-attn-LN out).
Added a **compile-time fusion pass** (`src/ep/ep.cc::CompileImpl`) that, for each group of `MatMulNBits`
sharing `input[0]`, **concatenates their int4 packed weights + scales along the output N dim ONCE**
into plan-owned buffers (`TensorRef::init_owned`), emits **one** fused GEMM, and splits the
`[M, N_total]` result back into the original outputs. A new internal handler
`mlx.fused::MatMulNBits` (`src/ep/ops/quant.cc`, shares `MatMulNBitsCore` with the plain op) does the
`mlx_quantized_matmul` + split. int4 block-quant concat along N is exact (rows independent;
block structure is per-(N, K/block)). Repack still runs **once** (cached on the fused weight name).

**Dispatch reduction:** 169 → **97** `MatMulNBits` (−72, −43%); repack-cache entries 507 → 291 (=97×3).
48 groups fused (24 layers × {qkv 3→1, gate/up 2→1}).

## Split had to be `mlx_slice_dynamic`
Shapeless `mlx_compile` (the decode fast-path) **cannot infer `Slice` or `Split` output shapes** with a
symbolic leading dim (`[Primitive::output_shapes] Slice/Split cannot infer output shapes`). `take_axis`
(gather) works but copies + adds dispatches (slower). **`mlx_slice_dynamic`** (runtime start, static
full-rank `slice_size=[M,Ni]`; M≡1 on the decode path) is the only shapeless-safe, contiguous split.

## Coherence (COHERENCE FIRST — PASS, byte-exact)
- `profile_decode` "The capital of France is" → "…Paris…" identical, FUSE on & off.
- `mlx_e2e` MLX token stream **byte-identical** FUSE=0 vs FUSE=1 (16 tokens):
  `59604 11 279 6722 315 9625 11 374 264 3283 7407 304 279 4126 315 279`.

## Tests
- `mlx_op_tests` (pytest tests/ops): **PASS**
- `mlx_leak_test`: **PASS** both configs (FUSE=1 growth 0.25 MiB ≪ 16 MiB tol) — fused path leak-clean.
- `mlx_e2e` coherence gate (MLX==CPU): **pre-existing FAIL**, unrelated to this change — fails
  IDENTICALLY on the clean baseline (stash-verified; identical MLX tokens). Not a regression.
  (Note: `tests/e2e/prompt_tokens.txt` fixture was missing; regenerated via `prepare_prompt.py`.)

## Performance A/B (M1 Max, warm, order-swapped, thermal-controlled)
Decode eval (in-EP profiler, avg over 12 windows, order-swapped ×3):
| config | eval us/fwd |
|--------|-------------|
| FUSE=0 | ~4700–4870  |
| FUSE=1 | ~5100–5380  |  → **decode REGRESSES ~5–10%**

Decode tok/s (mlx_e2e, best-of-8 prefill iters):
| config | prefill TTFT (24 tok) | decode tok/s |
|--------|----------------------|--------------|
| FUSE=0 | 14.77–14.84 ms       | ~156         |
| FUSE=1 | 14.01–14.08 ms       | ~150         |

→ **Prefill TTFT does NOT regress** (marginally better, ~5%). **Decode regresses ~4–6%.**

## Why it doesn't win on 0.5B (the honest finding)
At **M=1 decode each projection is a memory-bandwidth-bound GEMV**: fusing q/k/v reads the **same total
weight bytes**, so there is **no bandwidth win** — only kernel-launch savings. Those savings are smaller
than the cost of the mandatory split-back (you must produce the same N output tensors; `slice_dynamic`
adds ~5 ops/layer vs ~3 GEMV launches saved). Net: dispatch count is not the true decode bottleneck
here (weight-read bandwidth is), so fusion is a small net loss. It is **neutral/positive on prefill**
(M=S, compute-bound, better GEMM occupancy) and should **compound on larger models / longer prompts**.

## Decision
- **Ship default-OFF** to protect the decode hot path ("性能超级高" ⇒ don't regress decode).
- Keep the capability **gated + byte-exact** (`ONNX_GENAI_MLX_FUSE=1`) for A/B and larger models.
- Real decode wins for this model must attack **int4 weight-read bandwidth**, not dispatch count.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/py-binding.md -->
### 2026-07-13: Pip-installable Python binding (nanobind) for the MLX EP

**By:** build/packaging worker (branch `feat/python-binding`)

**What:** Added a `python/` package `onnxruntime_mlx` (distribution name
`onnxruntime-mlx`) that ships the plugin EP dylib + its mlx deps as a wheel and
registers the EP with a stock `onnxruntime` wheel. No changes to `src/ep/*`
behavior or the top-level CMake.

#### Package layout
```
python/
  pyproject.toml                # scikit-build-core backend + nanobind
  README.md
  CMakeLists.txt                # reuses top-level CMake via add_subdirectory(..)
  cmake/fixup_bundle.cmake.in   # install-time bundling + install_name rewrite + re-sign
  src/_core.cpp                 # nanobind ext: ep_name/version/vendor/library_path
  src/onnxruntime_mlx/
    __init__.py                 # register_execution_provider_library(), append_to_session_options()
    _core.pyi, py.typed
```

#### Build backend + commands
- Backend: **scikit-build-core** (`build-backend = "scikit_build_core.build"`),
  extension via **nanobind** (`nanobind_add_module`).
- Build deps installed once: `pip install nanobind scikit-build-core delocate`.
- The Python CMake does `add_subdirectory(<repo root>)` with
  `ORT_MLX_BUILD_E2E/TESTS=OFF` to build the existing `onnxruntime_mlx_ep` target,
  then builds `onnxruntime_mlx._core` and installs both into the wheel package.
- Repo root is auto-detected as the parent of `python/`; override with
  `-C cmake.define.ORT_MLX_REPO_ROOT=/abs/path`. pip 25.1 builds local dirs
  in-tree, so `..` is visible during the build.
- Install (dev): `cd python && MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0 pip install .`
  (or `pip install -e . --no-build-isolation`). Set
  `MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=14.0` or the wheel platform tag inherits the build
  host's macOS version (e.g. `macosx_26_0`).

#### How the dylib + mlx deps are bundled (self-contained wheel)
At install time `fixup_bundle.cmake` (run via `install(SCRIPT ...)`):
1. copies `libmlxc.dylib`, `libmlx.dylib`, `mlx.metallib` next to the plugin;
2. rewrites plugin mlx deps -> `@loader_path/lib{mlxc,mlx}.dylib`, and the
   bundled install ids to match (`libmlxc`'s dep on `libmlx` too);
3. rewrites the plugin's `onnxruntime` dep -> `@rpath/libonnxruntime.1.dylib`,
   resolved at runtime from the **host `onnxruntime` package already loaded
   in-process** (macOS two-level-namespace image match). Adds rpath
   `@loader_path/../onnxruntime/capi` as a fallback. `onnxruntime` is
   deliberately NOT vendored (must match the host).
4. **re-signs** each modified binary ad-hoc (`codesign --force --sign -`).
   Critical: on Apple Silicon `install_name_tool` invalidates the signature and
   dyld SIGKILLs the process (exit 137) without a re-sign.

`mlx.metallib` (~130 MB) must be colocated with `libmlx.dylib`; MLX locates its
default metallib via dladdr next to the dylib. `delocate` does NOT copy the
metallib, so bundling is done in CMake (not delocate) to get a truly
self-contained wheel. For CI, `cibuildwheel` repair uses
`delocate-wheel -e libonnxruntime` (exclude onnxruntime) as belt-and-suspenders.

#### Register API
```python
import onnxruntime as ort
import onnxruntime_mlx
onnxruntime_mlx.register_execution_provider_library()          # -> ort.register_execution_provider_library("MLXExecutionProvider", library_path())
sess = ort.InferenceSession(model, providers=["MLXExecutionProvider", "CPUExecutionProvider"])
```
Exports: `library_path()`, `ep_name()` (`"MLXExecutionProvider"`), `version()`
(`"0.1.0"`), `vendor()`, `register_execution_provider_library(name=...)`,
`append_to_session_options(so, name=...)`.

#### Verified
- `pip install .` and `pip wheel .` both succeed (nanobind ext + bundled dylib).
- `python -c "import onnxruntime_mlx as m; print(m.version(), m.ep_name(), m.library_path())"`
  -> `0.1.0 MLXExecutionProvider <site-packages>/onnxruntime_mlx/libonnxruntime_mlx_ep.dylib`.
- **E2E:** register + `InferenceSession(qwen2.5-0.5b, providers=[MLX,CPU])` +
  greedy decode -> `session.get_providers()` includes `MLXExecutionProvider`,
  output **"The capital of France is Paris."** No `DYLD_LIBRARY_PATH` needed:
  the pip `onnxruntime==1.27.0` wheel is loaded first and the plugin's
  `@rpath/libonnxruntime.1.dylib` binds to it.
- **onnxruntime version:** works with the pip `onnxruntime==1.27.0` wheel
  (exposes `register_execution_provider_library`, plugin-EP C API,
  `ORT_API_VERSION 27`). Declared floor `onnxruntime>=1.22`.
- Top-level `cmake --build build` + full `ctest` (mlx_op_tests, mlx_leak_test,
  mlx_e2e) still pass; no `src/ep/*` or root CMake changes.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/rachael-logprobs-server.md -->
### 2026-07-12: Expose engine logprobs through OpenAI server responses
**By:** Rachael
**What:** `/v1/chat/completions` now accepts `logprobs` plus `top_logprobs` (0–20) and returns OpenAI `choices[].logprobs.content` token records with UTF-8 bytes and top alternatives. `/v1/completions` accepts integer `logprobs` (0–5) and returns legacy `tokens`, `token_logprobs`, `top_logprobs`, and `text_offset` arrays. Both SSE endpoints emit the corresponding per-token logprobs shapes.
**Why:** The engine already exposes chosen-token natural-log probabilities and requested top alternatives through `GenerateResult::logprobs`, so the server can map real tokenizer-decoded values without fabrication. The remaining engine API gap is that `GenerateToken` streaming callback events do not carry logprobs; requests for streaming logprobs therefore wait for the final `GenerateResult` and then emit token-shaped SSE chunks, while streams without logprobs retain immediate token delivery.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/roy-metal-ep-design.md -->
### 2026-07-12: Metal/MPS ONNX Runtime plugin-EP — architecture + phased plan (design doc)

**By:** Roy
**What:** Wrote the design doc for a new initiative in the `onnxruntime-mps` repo
(`docs/DESIGN.md`) plus a `README.md` and repo skeleton (`src/ep`, `src/kernels`, `include`,
`cmake`, `tests`). The plan: build a custom **Apple Metal/MPS execution provider** for ONNX
Runtime as an **out-of-tree plugin EP** using ORT's plugin-EP **C ABI**
(`include/onnxruntime/core/session/onnxruntime_ep_c_api.h`), shipped as a standalone
`libonnxruntime_mps_ep.dylib` that the **stock prebuilt ORT 1.27** (`ORT_API_VERSION 27`, the
version `onnx-genai/ort-sys` already downloads: `onnxruntime-osx-arm64-1.27.0`) loads via
`RegisterExecutionProviderLibrary` + `SessionOptionsAppendExecutionProvider_V2` — **no ORT
fork, no ORT rebuild**. The `.dylib` exports `CreateEpFactories`/`ReleaseEpFactory`; we
implement `MetalEpFactory : OrtEpFactory`, `MetalEp : OrtEp`, a shared-storage `MTLBuffer`
allocator, and an `OrtDataTransferImpl`. Approach is **kernel-based** (register per-op kernels,
`AddSingleNode` in `GetCapability`, unclaimed nodes fall back to the ORT CPU EP), with a
compile-based fusion path held as a Phase-3 perf lever.

Operator coverage is derived from the graphs onnx-genai actually runs
(`models/qwen2.5-0.5b-cpu-recipe/model.onnx` — 394 nodes) and Mobius emission. **P0 hot path:**
`com.microsoft::MatMulNBits` (169; int4 block_size=32 accuracy_level=4 → int8-accumulated
matmul), `com.microsoft::GroupQueryAttention` (24; num_heads=14, kv_num_heads=2, do_rotary=1 —
fused RoPE+KV+causal/sliding-window flash attention), `RMSNormalization` /
`SkipSimplifiedLayerNormalization` (48), `Softmax`, RoPE. **P1:** `GatherBlockQuantized` (int4
embedding), `MatMul` fp16, elementwise `Add`/`Mul`/`Sub`/`Sigmoid`/`SiLU`/`Cast`. **P2/P3:**
shape/index ops + broader Mobius families (`RotaryEmbedding`, `LayerNormalization`, `Gelu`,
`Where`, `Split`, `Conv`, `Attention`, …). Kernels are fp16 compute / fp32-accumulate,
simdgroup-tiled, referencing **llama.cpp `ggml-metal`**, **ExecuTorch `backends/apple/mps`**,
and **PyTorch `aten/src/ATen/native/mps`**. Apple **unified memory** is the core advantage:
KV lives in a shared `MTLBuffer` with **no host↔device round-trip** (the exact cost that caps
our WebGPU decode).

**Testing** runs through onnx-genai: add an `ONNX_GENAI_EP=metal` path (feature-gated like
`cuda`) that registers the plugin library, per-kernel correctness vs the ORT CPU reference, a
coherence gate ("capital of France" ⇒ "Paris" before any perf claim), and a cross-runtime
benchmark (`scripts/compare_runtimes.sh`) vs Foundry Local / LM Studio / Ollama Metal.

**Phased plan:** P1 EP skeleton + fallback + elementwise/RMSNorm (Nabil EP, Mariette kernels);
P2 decode hot path — MatMulNBits GEMV, GQA, SkipSimplifiedLayerNorm, Softmax,
GatherBlockQuantized, shared-KV MTLBuffer (Mariette/Coco kernels, Nabil KV binding); P3 prefill
GEMM (`simdgroup_matrix`), fusion, sliding-window, full coverage + perf (Freysa bench).
**Ownership:** Nabil = EP integration/ABI/partitioning; Mariette = compute kernels; Coco =
data/quant kernels (int4/int8 numerics); Freysa = perf/test; Roy = architecture/review.

**Why:** onnx-genai is provably **ORT-kernel-bound on Apple Silicon** — the CPU int4 recipe
edges LM Studio short but trails ~28% long and trails Foundry Local both ways; WebGPU trails CPU
because KV round-trips host↔device (device-KV SIGSEGVs on ORT 1.27 WebGPU). ORT's generic
int4 CPU/WebGPU kernels cannot match llama.cpp's hand-tuned Metal `mul_mv_q4`. A plugin EP lets
us drop our own Metal kernels into the same ORT session our runtime already drives, without
forking ORT, targeting the fastest-runtime-on-Mac goal.

**Blocking unknowns for Justin:** (1) confirm the stock `onnxruntime-osx-arm64-1.27.0` binary is
a **non-minimal** build that exports `RegisterExecutionProviderLibrary`/`GetEpApi`/
`SessionOptionsAppendExecutionProvider_V2` (one `nm -gU` check; if minimal we need a self-built
or non-minimal ORT — a scope decision); (2) pin **1.27.0** (runtime parity, recommended) vs
public **1.27.1** (same API 27); (3) confirm P1–P2 target **only** the Qwen2.5-decoder op set,
deferring Conv/Attention/VLM ops. Also open: Apple `OrtHardwareDevice` enumeration may need
`CreateHardwareDevice` to synthesize a virtual Metal GPU device.

**Files created:** `onnxruntime-mps/docs/DESIGN.md`, `onnxruntime-mps/README.md`, skeleton dirs
`src/ep`, `src/kernels`, `include/onnxruntime_mps`, `cmake`, `tests/{kernels,e2e}` (`.gitkeep`).
Design doc only — **no kernel/EP implementation code** this phase (that is P1 for
Nabil/Mariette/Coco).
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sapper-399-rebase.md -->
### 2026-07-13: Preserve mixed Q4_K_M weights and quantized embeddings
**By:** Sapper
**What:** Rebased Mobius PR #399 onto #400 and made quantized linear modules normalize every mixed GGUF projection to the graph's common MatMulNBits layout, while compatible token embeddings remain packed for GatherBlockQuantized.
**Why:** Q4_K_M contains source types that require dequantization and requantization, but embedding preservation must remain metadata-only and exclude Tencent's custom Q1_0 layout. The combined path retains both features without misleading tensor-count logging.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sapper-401-rebase-review.md -->
### 2026-07-13: Guard GGUF requantization targets in Mobius PR #401
**By:** Sapper
**What:** Rebased `feat/gguf-quantized-head` onto Mobius `origin/main`, preserving Q4_K_M and quantized-embedding behavior. Requantization now explicitly rejects targets other than 4-bit/block-32 with a tensor-specific actionable error, and the loader log reports state-dict entries plus GGUF tensor counters without deriving a false dequantized count.
**Why:** `repack_dequantized_tensor()` only implements 4-bit/block-32 output, while packed state-dict tensors expand into multiple entries and cannot be used to infer the number of dequantized GGUF tensors.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sapper-pr-review-fixes.md -->
### 2026-07-12: Address Mobius GGUF review findings
**By:** Sapper
**What:** PR #400 now checks embedding quantization compatibility from GGUF tensor metadata and rejects Tencent Q1_0 embeddings without repacking. PR #399 now labels `len(state_dict)` as state-dict entries and no longer derives a dequantized tensor count from it. Stacked PR #401 was rebased onto the updated PR #400 branch.
**Why:** Avoid duplicate full embedding repacks and Tencent Q1_0 size failures, keep conversion logging dimensionally accurate, and preserve a clean stacked branch relationship.
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sapper-quantized-head.md -->
### 2026-07-12: Preserve GGUF output heads with MatMulNBits
**By:** Sapper
**What:** Mobius `build-gguf --keep-quantized` now emits quantized untied `output.weight` as `MatMulNBits`, normalizing mixed source types such as Q8_0/Q6_K to the graph's Q4 block-32 layout. Tied heads continue to share the quantized embedding table through `GatherBlockQuantized` plus `MatMulNBits`. The Qwen2.5-0.5B Q4_0 build has 169 `MatMulNBits` nodes, one `GatherBlockQuantized`, no plain `MatMul`, and no 544 MB fp32 embedding/head initializers; CPU output coherently begins “Paris”.
**Why:** The prior untied fp32 output head read 544 MB per decoded token. The new model shrinks from 1.2 GB to 388 MB, with packed head weights occupying 68 MB plus 17 MB of scales. A quick 6-thread direct-engine check measured 38.18 tok/s versus 39.80 tok/s for the old model, so ORT's very-wide Q4 `MatMulNBits` did not improve throughput in this small sample despite eliminating the memory-heavy fp32 head.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sebastian-accuracy-level-debug.md -->
# Root cause: ORT MatMulNBits 3.6x slower than llama.cpp = **missing `accuracy_level`** (slow fp32 MLAS path)

**By:** Sebastian (Performance)
**Date:** 2026-07-12
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, 10 cores (8P+2E), macOS 25.5.0
**Runtime:** onnx-genai CPU EP, ORT **1.24.3** (env `onnx`); ORT 1.27 semantics identical for `accuracy_level`.
**Base model:** `models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-onnx-fixed` (168 Q4 `MatMulNBits`)

## TL;DR — hypothesis CONFIRMED, gap CLOSED

The user was right to be skeptical of "the ORT kernel is just slow." Our Mobius
models emit `MatMulNBits {K, N, bits:4, block_size:32}` with **no `accuracy_level`
attribute**. Unset ⇒ ORT's MLAS CPU kernel runs the **fp32 dequant + fp32 GEMM**
path. `accuracy_level=4` selects the **int8 dynamic-quant + int8 dot-product**
path (ARM SDOT/NEON) — the same class of kernel llama.cpp uses. ORT's parity
claim holds; we were simply not opting into the fast path.

Setting `accuracy_level=4` on every `MatMulNBits`:

| model (CPU EP, 64-tok decode, warmup+3 runs) | threads | decode tok/s | coherent? |
|---|---|---:|:--:|
| baseline `q4-onnx-fixed` (accuracy_level **MISSING**) | ORT default | **39.3** | ✅ Paris |
| **`accuracy_level=4` (int8)** | ORT default | **91.8** (2.33×) | ✅ Paris |
| `accuracy_level=2` (fp16) | ORT default | 34.8 (slower) | ✅ |
| `accuracy_level=3` (bf16) | ORT default | 35.4 (slower) | ✅ |
| quantized head only (`q4-head`, no accuracy_level) | ORT default | 41.8 | ✅ |
| **quantized head + `accuracy_level=4`** | ORT default | **194.7** 🎯 | ✅ Paris |
| quantized head + `accuracy_level=4` | 6 (pinned) | 157.0 | ✅ |
| **Mobius `build-gguf --ep cpu` (fix baked in)** | ORT default | **99.0** (2.5×) | ✅ Paris |
| — reference: LM Studio CPU (Sebastian prior note) | — | 157 | ✅ |

- **`accuracy_level=4` alone** closes ~60% of the 3.6× gap (3.6× → ~1.6×).
- **`accuracy_level=4` + quantized head** fully closes and **exceeds** LM Studio
  CPU (195 tok/s default threads; 157 at 6 pinned threads). Output bit-identical
  to baseline → int8 accuracy loss is not observable here.
- **fp16/bf16 (levels 2/3) regress** on M1 — no native fp16/bf16 GEMM; skip them.

## Why the earlier "quantizing the head gave no speedup" observation happened

The `q4-head` model's head is a `MatMulNBits` too — and it was **also missing
`accuracy_level`**, so it stayed on the slow fp32 path (41.8 tok/s, ~unchanged).
The head is the single largest matmul (`151936×896`), so once `accuracy_level=4`
lets it use int8, it delivers the biggest jump (92 → 195). The two fixes are
multiplicative and both were gated behind the same missing attribute.

## Usual suspects — cleared

- **Graph optimization:** `session.rs` `optimization_level: 99` → `ORT_ENABLE_ALL`. Already max. Not the issue.
- **Threads:** 6–8 P-cores optimal; 10 (incl. E-cores) ~2× slower. Already handled by `ONNX_GENAI_INTRA_OP_THREADS` (prior note). Not the root cause.
- **Prepacking / session persistence / orchestration:** prior profile showed 98.9% in `session.run`; those were already ruled out.
- **ARM MLAS kernel present?** Yes — the 2.33× jump from `accuracy_level=4` proves the int8 SDOT/NEON `MatMulNBits` kernel exists and is being used; we just weren't selecting it.

## THE FIX (Mobius) — implemented

Branch: **`perf/matmulnbits-accuracy-level`** (off `main`, in `/Users/justinc/Documents/GitHub/mobius`; NOT committed — coordinator commits).

`default_int4_accuracy_level=4` already existed on the `cpu`/`webgpu`
`EpCapabilities` (`_execution_providers.py`) but was **dead config** — never read.
Plumbed it into the `MatMulNBits` emission:

- `src/mobius/components/_quantized_linear.py`
  - Added `_accuracy_level_attrs()` helper reading `ep_capabilities().default_int4_accuracy_level`; emits `accuracy_level=<n>` when `n>0`, omits it when `0` (preserves default/portable behavior).
  - Applied at **both** emission sites: `QuantizedLinear.forward` (Q/K/V/O + MLP + non-tied LM head via the factory) and `TiedQuantizedLMHead.forward`.
  - Central at graph-build time via the existing `_build_context`, so all factory call sites in `models/base.py` are covered without constructor plumbing.
- `src/mobius/components/_quantized_linear_test.py`
  - Updated `test_matmulnbits_attributes` to assert default omits `accuracy_level`.
  - Added `test_no_accuracy_level_without_context` and `test_cpu_ep_emits_accuracy_level_4`.

**End-to-end verified:** `mobius build-gguf <q4_0.gguf> --ep cpu --keep-quantized`
→ all 168 `MatMulNBits` carry `accuracy_level=4`; `--ep default` correctly omits
it (no regression). The `--ep cpu` model benchmarks at **99 tok/s (2.5×)**,
coherent.

### Follow-up for Sapper
The compounding win needs the **quantized embedding + head** on the CPU build
(the `GatherBlockQuantized` + tied/quantized `MatMulNBits` head, per Sapper's
quantized-head work) shipped *together with* `--ep cpu`. That combination is what
hits ~157–195 tok/s. The Mobius `--ep cpu` default now carries `accuracy_level=4`
for both the head and projections automatically, so Sapper's quantized-head path
gets it for free once merged. Recommend the CPU model recipe = quantized
head/embedding **+** `--ep cpu`.

## Validation (exit codes)
- Mobius: `lintrunner` changed files → **ok, 0 issues**; `pytest src/mobius/components/ src/mobius/models/base.py` → **518 passed**; targeted `_quantized_linear_test.py` → **41 passed**.
- onnx-genai: `cargo clippy -p onnx-genai-bench --all-targets --features bench-ort -- -D warnings` → **exit 0**. (onnx-genai change is harness-only: `profile_decode.rs` now prints generated text for coherence checks. No production runtime change is required — the fix is entirely model-side in Mobius.)

## Files changed (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)
**mobius** (branch `perf/matmulnbits-accuracy-level`, off main):
- `src/mobius/components/_quantized_linear.py`
- `src/mobius/components/_quantized_linear_test.py`

**onnx-genai** (harness only, optional):
- `crates/onnx-genai-bench/src/bin/profile_decode.rs` — print generated text for coherence verification.

Helper (not for commit): `models/.scratch/patch_accuracy_level.py` (post-hoc attribute patcher used for the A/B test).

## How to reproduce
```bash
# Build the fixed model straight from GGUF (fix baked in):
cd mobius && PYTHONPATH=$PWD/src python -m mobius build-gguf \
  <path>/qwen2.5-0.5b-instruct-q4_0.gguf --output <out> --keep-quantized --ep cpu
# -> every MatMulNBits has accuracy_level=4

# Benchmark (copy tokenizer/genai_config from an existing qwen2.5 q4 build first):
ONNX_GENAI_EP=cpu ./target/release/profile_decode \
  --model <out> --tokens 64 --warmups 1 --runs 3 --prompt "The capital of France is"

# A/B a pre-built model without rebuilding:
python models/.scratch/patch_accuracy_level.py \
  models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-onnx-fixed models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-acc4 4
```


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sebastian-cpu-profile.md -->
# CPU decode profiling: onnx-genai is ORT-kernel-bound, not orchestration-bound

**By:** Sebastian (Performance)
**Date:** 2026-07-12
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, 10 cores (8 performance + 2 efficiency), macOS 25.5.0, ORT 1.27.0
**Model:** `models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-onnx-fixed` (168 Q4 `MatMulNBits` projections, 24 `Attention`)

## TL;DR

Justin's hypothesis was: if we're slower than LM Studio **CPU-vs-CPU**, the
bottleneck is inside our runtime, not the EP. I profiled the decode path.
**The bottleneck is ORT's kernel compute, not our orchestration.** ~98.9% of
per-token wall time is inside `session.run`; our entire orchestration layer
(tensor binding, KV rotation, logits copy, sampling, detokenization) is ~1%.
Even with perfect orchestration we cannot close a 3.6x gap that lives inside the
ORT kernels. There is one large **model-side** lever (a 544 MB fp32 output-head
matmul every token) that is addressable via Mobius.

## 1. CPU-vs-CPU confirmed (this Mac, same source GGUF)

LM Studio forced to CPU (`lms load … --gpu off`), onnx-genai CPU EP, both on the
exact same SHA-256 Q4_0 GGUF (`7671c0c3…`), 1 warmup + 5 runs, 64 tokens, greedy:

| prompt | runtime | decode tok/s | TTFT ms | total ms |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| short (59) | onnx-genai | **43.6** | 170 | 1620 |
| short (59) | LM Studio CPU | **157.1** | 61 | 458 |
| long (858) | onnx-genai | **41.0** | 2199 | 3733 |
| long (858) | LM Studio CPU | **155.7** | 68 | 467 |

We are **72–74% slower** in decode (~3.6x), and TTFT/prefill is far worse
(long-prompt TTFT is 32x). Both produce coherent output ("The capital of France
is Paris. It is the largest city in Europe…"). This confirms and updates the
prior "~40 vs ~141" note: LM Studio CPU is now ~156 tok/s, ours ~43.

## 2. Per-stage decode profile (attribution)

Added an env-gated profiler (`ONNX_GENAI_PROFILE=1`) and a direct-engine harness
(`profile_decode`), 128-token decode × 3 runs, default threads:

| stage | us/token | % of token | what it is |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| **`ort.session_run`** | **23,532** | **98.9%** | pure ORT compute (`run_with_binding`) |
| `loop.sampling` | 102 | 0.43% | argmax over 151,936-vocab logits |
| `engine.logits_to_vec` | 50 | 0.21% | copy logits Value → `Vec<f32>` (151,936 f32) |
| `ort.bind_inputs` | 43 | 0.18% | build + bind input tensors |
| `ort.extract_outputs` | 35 | 0.15% | extract logits/KV from binding, rotate |
| `loop.detokenize` | 3.3 | 0.01% | `tokenizer.decode` |
| `loop.commit_token` | 0.3 | ~0% | push token, scheduler advance |

Per-token wall ≈ 23,779 us → **42 tok/s**. Everything outside `session.run` sums
to **~230 us/token (~1%)**. HTTP/SSE overhead is not in this loop; the server's
end-to-end tok/s (43.6) matches the direct harness (42), so SSE/HTTP adds
negligible per-token cost too.

**Verdict: the gap is ORT-kernel-bound, not our-orchestration-bound.**

## 3. The usual suspects, checked

- **(a) Intra-op thread count** — real but not the fix on this machine.
  Thread sweep (96-token decode):

  | threads | tok/s |
  |---:|---:|
  | 1 | 12.0 |
  | 4 | 36.3 |
  | **6** | **46.0** |
  | 8 | 45.8 |
  | 10 | 22.7 |
  | ORT default | 37–44 |

  6–8 (performance) cores are optimal; **forcing all 10 (incl. the 2 efficiency
  cores) is ~2x slower** — the fast cores stall on the slow ones. ORT's default
  lands ~37–44 (variable in a standalone process, ~44 in the long-lived server —
  already near the 6-thread optimum, so pinning did **not** move the server
  number). Net: a tuning/safety knob, not a decode win here.
- **(b) Per-token host KV copies / reallocations** — not a problem. The
  `DecodeSession` runner rotates KV via `Arc` (no data copy); inputs are bound
  through `IoBinding`. KV growth cost is inside ORT, not our code.
- **(c) Redundant prefill / attention_mask growth** — decode sends only the new
  token (`input_ids` len 1) with a full `[1, past+1]` mask; no prefill recompute.
  The growing mask is correct and cheap (`bind_inputs` = 43 us).
- **(d) MatMulNBits CPU kernel efficiency** — this **is** the gap (inside
  `session_run`), see §4.
- **(e) Per-token allocations in logit/sampling path** — measured at ~150 us
  total (logits copy 50 + sampling 102). Real but ~0.6%; not worth chasing now.

## 4. Top fixable bottlenecks

1. **fp32 output head (biggest addressable, model-side).** The fixed model
   carries an **untied `lm_head.weight_t` as a plain fp32 `MatMul`**:
   `[151936, 896]` FLOAT32 = **544 MB**, plus a separate 544 MB fp32
   `embed_tokens`. Every decode token runs `[1,896]×[896,151936]` in fp32,
   reading ~544 MB of weights — at ~100 GB/s that alone is ~5 ms/token, roughly
   **~23% of the 23.5 ms/token**. llama.cpp keeps this head quantized
   (Q6_K/Q8_0, 4–8x smaller). **Fix:** build the CPU model with the quantized
   embedding + head (the CUDA stacked path's `GatherBlockQuantized` +
   `--keep-quantized`, per Sapper's notes) instead of expanding to fp32. This is
   a Mobius change, not a runtime change, and is the single clearest CPU lever.
2. **ORT `MatMulNBits` / MLAS CPU kernel vs llama.cpp (the remaining gap).**
   After the output head, the residual is the 168 Q4 `MatMulNBits` projections +
   attention. This is ORT's MLAS int4 kernel vs llama.cpp's hand-tuned
   ARM/AVX GGML kernels. **This is a different class of problem** — we don't own
   the kernel. Options: ensure the ORT build uses the best MLAS ISA path, try a
   faster EP (WebGPU/CoreML/CUDA on the H200), or accept the CPU deficit.
3. **Intra-op thread pinning (implemented, low-risk knob).** See §5.

Sampling argmax (102 us) and the logits copy (50 us) are minor and left as-is.

## 5. Quick win implemented: `ONNX_GENAI_INTRA_OP_THREADS`

Added an env-gated intra-op thread override in `onnx-genai-ort` session
creation. It only applies when the caller left threads at the default
(`with_intra_op_threads(n>0)` — used by exact-equality tests — always wins), and
rejects non-positive/invalid values. This lets operators pin the ORT pool to the
performance-core count and **avoid the measured ~2x regression when ORT picks a
bad count** (e.g. all 10 cores on M1 Max, or hyperthread oversubscription on
Linux). On this Mac the long-lived server already defaults near the optimum, so
the HTTP decode number was unchanged (43.6 → 43.95) — the value is robustness and
tunability, not a headline speedup. Re-measured green.

## 6. Files changed (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)

- `crates/onnx-genai-ort/src/profile.rs` **(new)** — env-gated per-stage profiler.
- `crates/onnx-genai-ort/src/lib.rs` — export `pub mod profile`.
- `crates/onnx-genai-ort/src/decode.rs` — spans around `bind_inputs`,
  `session_run`, `extract_outputs`.
- `crates/onnx-genai-ort/src/session.rs` — `ONNX_GENAI_INTRA_OP_THREADS`
  override (`intra_op_threads_from_env`), explicit setting still wins.
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/decode.rs` — span around `logits_to_vec`.
- `crates/onnx-genai-engine/src/decode_loop.rs` — spans around next_logits,
  sampling, commit_token, commit_selected, detokenize.
- `crates/onnx-genai-bench/src/bin/profile_decode.rs` **(new)** +
  `crates/onnx-genai-bench/Cargo.toml` `[[bin]]` decl — direct-engine profiling
  harness (requires `--features bench-ort`).

Validation: `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` → exit 0;
`cargo test -p onnx-genai-ort -p onnx-genai-engine` → 0 failed.

## 7. How to reproduce

```bash
# Per-stage profile (direct engine, no HTTP):
ONNX_GENAI_PROFILE=1 cargo run --release -p onnx-genai-bench \
  --features bench-ort --bin profile_decode -- \
  --model models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-onnx-fixed --tokens 128 --warmups 1 --runs 3

# Thread sweep:
for t in 1 4 6 8 10; do ./target/release/profile_decode \
  --model models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-onnx-fixed --tokens 96 --runs 2 --threads $t; done

# Server tuned:
ONNX_GENAI_EP=cpu ONNX_GENAI_INTRA_OP_THREADS=6 \
  cargo run --release -p onnx-genai-server -- \
  --model models/qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-onnx-fixed --model-id qwen2.5-0.5b-q4-cpu --addr 127.0.0.1:8080
```
<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sebastian-cpu-recipe-bench.md -->
### 2026-07-12: Treat the full CPU recipe as competitive, not a universal llama.cpp win
**By:** Sebastian
**What:** The correctness-verified Mobius CPU recipe (169 int4 `MatMulNBits` nodes at `accuracy_level=4`, quantized embedding/head) measures 158.62/164.42 tok/s median/p90 on the 59-token prompt and 115.19/119.42 on the 858-token prompt. It beats Ollama CPU decode in both cases and edges LM Studio CPU by 2.0% short, but trails LM Studio by 28.1% long. Foundry Local CPU reaches 202.67/212.88 short and 165.77/169.33 long with a 121-node int4 graph using fused QKV and a fp32 embedding.
**Why:** The 39.3 → 91.8 → 194.7 direct-probe trajectory proves `accuracy_level=4` plus the quantized head fixed the dominant model-side decode bottleneck, but the definitive API workload exposes remaining long-context prefill/KV costs and a graph-packing gap. Do not claim that onnx-genai broadly beats llama.cpp CPU yet; prioritize CPU prefill and packed QKV, using Foundry Local as the direct ORT-vs-ORT target.


<!-- merged from .squad/decisions/inbox/sebastian-foundry-analysis.md -->
# Foundry Local is NOT faster than onnx-genai on CPU decode — the gap was pre-fix + thermal

**By:** Sebastian (Performance)
**Date:** 2026-07-13
**Machine:** Apple M1 Max, macOS 26.5.1
**Runtime:** onnx-genai CPU EP, ORT 1.27; Foundry Local SDK 1.2.3 (onnxruntime-genai, ORT 1.26)

## Decisive experiment (Task A): run FL's exact CPU model.onnx through OUR runtime

I downloaded Foundry Local's `qwen2.5-0.5b-instruct-generic-cpu:4` (SHA-256
`997228…cd21`, byte-identical to the 2026-07-12 benchmark's FL model), pointed
onnx-genai's CPU EP at it (hand-written `inference_metadata.yaml`; FL has no
`position_ids` input, which our binder tolerates), and got coherent output.

- **OURS-on-FL-model ≈ OURS-on-our-model ≈ FL-on-FL-model.** Direct-engine decode,
  interleaved: FL model ~215 tok/s, our model ~206 tok/s. Warm HTTP: short decode
  **211.8 (ours) vs 212.1 (FL)**; long decode **175.0 (ours) vs 159.8 (FL)**.
- **Conclusion: decode parity.** FL's model structure gives no advantage our
  runtime is missing, and the `MatMulNBits accuracy_level=4` kernel is at parity.
  On long context onnx-genai now **leads** FL (the fp32-GQA shared-KV-buffer fix
  in `decode.rs` landed; the old 115 tok/s was the growing-KV bug).

## Graph diff (Task A.3): fused QKV is real but decode-neutral on CPU

FL packs Q/K/V into one `MatMulNBits` (N=1152) → **121 MatMulNBits / 299 nodes**
vs our **169 / 394** (three separate 896/128/128), i.e. **48 fewer matmul
dispatches per token**. FL uses an fp32 `Gather` embedding; we use int4
`GatherBlockQuantized`. But fusing Q/K/V does not reduce weight bytes, and decode
`MatMulNBits` (M=1) is memory-bandwidth bound — so fusion only saves negligible
dispatch overhead. Measured: FL's 121-node model and our 169-node model decode at
the same speed. **Fused QKV is low-priority for CPU decode.**

## FL C++ orchestration (Task B): they tune nothing — they use onnxruntime-genai

`Foundry-Local/sdk_v2/cpp/src/` sets **no custom ORT SessionOptions** (no
intra/inter-op threads, graph-opt, mem arena, mem pattern, or config entries). It
delegates to onnxruntime-genai (`OgaModel`/`OgaGenerator`,
`inferencing/generative/genai_model_instance.cc:29-58`); IO binding and
`past_present_share_buffer` live in that library, not FL's code. The CPU
`genai_config.json` `decoder.session_options` is `{log_id, provider_options: []}`.
**We already match this posture** (`session.rs:59` optimization_level = ORT_ENABLE_ALL,
ORT-default threads; our own IO binding + shared-KV path). No missing session option.

## Why the 2026-07-12 gap did not reproduce

Machine variance is large (single unwarmed HTTP samples 85-216 tok/s; first request
is cold — ORT prepacking + arena + weight paging). With ≥2 discarded warmups our
server steadies at ~212 short. The prior 158.6 short / 115.2 long was measured
before the KV fix and under-warmed/throttled. FL was ~200-212 on both days.

## Decision / next steps

- **Do not treat Foundry Local as a CPU decode target to catch.** onnx-genai is at
  parity short and ahead long on FL's own model.
- **No runtime code change made this session** — none is warranted (no missing
  session option; already at parity). A speculative session tweak could regress.
- **Follow-ups:** (1) benchmark warmup discipline + optional server startup priming
  (Leon/Sebastian, low-risk); (2) TTFT/prefill is the only residual FL edge ~2-4%
  → chunked prefill (Leon); (3) fused-QKV in Mobius is low priority, prefill-only
  (Sapper).
- **Benchmark doc:** `docs/benchmarks/2026-07-13-foundry-local-analysis.md`.

## Files touched (coordinator to commit — I did NOT commit)
- `docs/benchmarks/2026-07-13-foundry-local-analysis.md` (new)
- `.squad/decisions/inbox/sebastian-foundry-analysis.md` (this file)
- `models/foundry-cpu/` (new; symlinks to FL cache model + hand-written
  `inference_metadata.yaml` + copied tokenizer — analysis scaffold, not a shipped model)
- `models/.scratch/{fl_download_cpu,fl_locate,fl_load,fl_serve,graph_analyze}.py` (analysis helpers)
